# MEMORY PALACE AND AAAK SHORTHAND

*Persistent cross-session memory organised into a navigable palace structure, with AAAK shorthand for efficient context loading. Adapted from the MemPalace architecture (MIT, github.com/milla-jovovich/mempalace) for local-first use. All memory stays on-device, passes through the privacy pipeline, and integrates with governance and audit.*

## Epic M1: Structured Memory System

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| M1.1 | XL | **Done** | Palace structure implementation (hierarchy: Wings → Halls → Rooms → Closets [AAAK summaries] → Drawers [verbatim] → Tunnels [cross-wing links]; auto-classification by local SLM; manual organisation; `loke memory walk`; SQLite + ChromaDB storage; `loke memory status`) |
| M1.2 | L | **Done** | Verbatim conversation storage (every turn stored verbatim in drawers, never summarised; classified into wing/hall/room; metadata: timestamp, models, tokens, session ID; pre-anonymisation content stored (stays local); conversation imports from Claude/ChatGPT/Slack exports; configurable retention and storage budget alerts) |
| M1.3 | L | **Done** | Semantic memory search (`loke memory search "why did we choose Clerk?"`; scoped by wing/hall; returns excerpts with context, source, timestamp, confidence; semantic similarity + palace structure boost; fully local embeddings; < 500ms for 100K drawers; integrated into browser chat panel) |
| M1.4 | L | **Done** | Automatic context enrichment (before every LLM call: search palace for relevant memories; top-N included as AAAK context; "Added N memories from palace" disclosure; user can disable; relevance threshold configurable; token budget cap default 2000; enrichment passes through anonymisation before cloud LLMs) |
| M1.5 | XL | **Done** | Knowledge graph with temporal awareness (entity extraction by local SLM; relationship mapping; temporal validity windows — facts have start/end dates; contradiction detection flags for user review; `loke memory facts "who works on project-alpha?"`; `--as-of` time queries; graph visualisation in browser mode; privacy pipeline on any externally-included graph data) |
| M1.6 | L | **Done** | Agent memory / diaries (each AG1 agent gets a dedicated palace wing; structured diary entries in AAAK after each run; diary loaded as context on startup; searchable; agent-scoped permissions; diary entries feed into project wings; older entries auto-compressed to closets) |
| M1.7 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **Four memory tables are never created** — Found 2026-09-23 while converting the row-accessor defects (LS1.1i). `memory_graph_entities`, `memory_graph_relations`, `memory_scores` and `memory_settings` are referenced **24 times** across `memory/graph.tk`, `memory/decay.tk` and `memory/privacy.tk`, and have **no `CREATE TABLE` anywhere in the repository**. `storage/migrations.tk` and `memory/schema.tk` between them create eight tables: `audit_log`, `memory_closets`, `memory_drawers`, `memory_index`, `placeholder_maps`, `schema_migrations`, `sessions`, `settings`. So the whole knowledge-graph path (M1.5), decay scoring and the memory privacy preferences write and read tables that do not exist. It was invisible because every read went through a JSON accessor and returned empty-or-zero regardless (LS1.1i) — fixing those accessors is what will surface this as a real error. Add the DDL with a migration, then assert each table exists in a test rather than inferring it from a query that returns nothing<br><br>**Done 2026-09-24.** All four tables now exist. `memory_scores` landed with the palace (migrations 10-16, under F10.23h/M1.7's neighbour work); `memory_graph_entities`, `memory_graph_relations` and `memory_settings` are migrations **17-19**, with indexes on the relation table's `from_id` and `to_id` at 21-22. Columns were derived from the queries that read them, not invented: `confidence` and `weight` are REAL because `memory/graph.tk` reads them through `s.rowf64hundredths` and divides by 100. Until now every read failed and the row accessors map a failed read to `0` or `""` (LS1.1i), so **every field of every memory-graph entity and relation was empty or zero and nothing reported it**. Found systematically rather than by inspection: `scripts/find_uncreated_tables.py` (new, in the gate) collects every table named after `FROM`/`INTO`/`UPDATE`/`JOIN` in a string literal and subtracts every `CREATE TABLE` anywhere in the tree — not just in migrations, because several modules legitimately create their own at runtime. It found **five** tables created nowhere, of which this row knew about four. |
| M1.7a | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **Nothing inserts into `memory_scores`** — found 2026-09-23 while closing M1.7. Repo-wide the table has one `UPDATE` (`accessed_at`), several `SELECT`s and a `DELETE`, and **no `INSERT` anywhere**. So the table now exists and stays empty: `core.memory.privacy`'s `WHERE pii_flagged=1` can never match, and `core.memory.decay`'s sweep has nothing to sweep. The export joins were written to cope (`COALESCE(ms.score,1.0)`), which is why nothing looked broken. A score row should be created when a drawer is created — `core.memory.storage.adddrawer` is the natural place, in the same transaction, so a drawer can never exist without a score. Decide whether `pii_flagged` is set there from the pipeline's detection result or by a later pass; setting it late means a window in which PII-bearing memories are not flagged. **Closed 2026-09-24:** `core.memory.storage.storedrawer` (there is no `adddrawer`) now writes the drawer and its score row between `BEGIN` and `COMMIT`, rolling back if either INSERT fails. **Decision:** `pii_flagged` is set there from the caller's sensitivity (CONFIDENTIAL or RESTRICTED gives 1, INTERNAL and PUBLIC give 0), so there is no unflagged window. `created_at` is bound as epoch seconds because `decay.sweeprows` reads it with `rowi64` (see M1.7c). `tests/unit/memory/test_drawer_scores.tk` runs against a real migrated store: 6 of its 12 assertions failed before the change, including the atomicity case (a clashing score id left an orphan drawer), and all pass after. Premise note: `memory/export.tk`'s import path did have an INSERT. |
| M1.7b | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **A pre-existing `datadir/memory.db` is no longer read** — `core.memory.palace.open` opened its own `memory.db` until 2026-09-23, which was not merely untidy: `std.db` keeps ONE global connection (T-30), so opening it silently replaced the process's connection and `audit_log` and `settings` ceased to exist for every reader afterwards. The palace now uses `loke.db`, the same file `core.storage.db.openstore` opens, and the four `memory_*` tables are created by migrations 10-16. Anyone who ran an earlier build still has `memory.db` on disk with drawers in it; nothing deletes it and nothing migrates it. Decide: a one-shot migration that copies `memory_drawers`/`memory_closets`/`memory_index` across and renames the file, or a release note saying the old file is orphaned. Given that no release has shipped and the palace's write path was itself broken (M1.7a — no score rows), a release note is probably honest and sufficient; the point of this row is that the choice is recorded rather than assumed. **Closed 2026-09-24. Decision: no migration.** The orphaned file cannot hold drawers. Every committed `palace.open` that used `memory.db` (up to `3ead30d~1`) called `schema.createtables`, whose five statements were `db.exec(conn;"CREATE ...")`. That passes the connection as the SQL, and it segfaulted on the first statement (measured, recorded in `tests/unit/memory/test_palace_drawers.tk`). So no build created `memory_drawers` in `memory.db`. No release or tag exists. The decision and its reason are recorded at `core.memory.palace.open`. Nothing deletes a stray `memory.db`. It holds no memory tables, so it holds no memories. |
| M1.7c | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **The decay sweep reads a datetime TEXT `created_at` as the year** — found 2026-09-24 while closing M1.7a. `core.memory.decay.sweeprows` reads `memory_scores.created_at` with `db.rowi64`, which is `strtoll` underneath. The column's default is `datetime('now')`, and `strtoll("2026-09-24 10:00:00")` is **2026**, which is 1970 plus about half an hour. `agereadable` accepts it, so any such row is aged about 56 years and decays straight to `minscore`. The default config's `minscore` (0.1) is above its `prunebelow` (0.05), so it clamps rather than deletes, but any configuration with `prunebelow` at or above `minscore` would prune every such row. `storedrawer` now binds epoch seconds (M1.7a). The import in `memory/export.tk` (`INSERT OR IGNORE INTO memory_scores`) does not set `created_at`, so imported rows get the text default. Fix: make one representation authoritative, either by migrating the column to INTEGER epoch or by having decay parse the text, and have `agereadable` reject a value below a plausible epoch floor.<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-24.** Decay now parses both representations: `decay.sweepsql` normalises `created_at` in the query (all digits is epoch seconds; anything else goes through `strftime('%s',…)`, which yields NULL, read as 0 and skipped, for text it cannot parse). `agereadable` rejects anything below `epochfloor()` (2020-01-01), so a mis-read year authorises no delete. Evidence: `tests/unit/memory/test_decay_created_at.tk` seeds `memory_scores` in a real migrated store with default-TEXT, 400-day-old TEXT, epoch, `'2026'` and `'now()'` rows and runs the real `decay.sweep` with `prunebelow` above `minscore`. **Seen red** against the unfixed module: 3 failures — the second-old TEXT row was pruned, the `'2026'` row was pruned, `agereadable(2026)` was true. Green after. `test_decay.tk`'s `agereadable` cases moved to real epochs. The import path still leaves the default, which now reads correctly as the import time; carrying the exported timestamp through is in LS1.1k. |
| M1.7d | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **`core.memory.storage.listdrawers` always returns an empty list** — found 2026-09-24 while closing M1.7a. `parselistdrawerrows` builds a `$drawer` literal in the loop body as a bare expression and never pushes it: `drawers` is `@()` at the start and `<$ok(drawers)` at the end, and nothing in between assigns it (T-24: `x=x.push(y)`). Every listing of a palace location is empty whatever the table holds. Fix and assert with a real store, the way `test_drawer_scores.tk` does.<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-24.** `parselistdrawerrows` now assigns `drawers=drawers.push(...)`, and `listdrawers` takes `location:$palacelocation` — it was declared `i64` and read `.wing`/`.hall`/`.room` off it, which toke accepted. Evidence: `tests/unit/memory/test_listdrawers.tk` stores three drawers through the real `storedrawer` into a migrated `loke.db` and asserts the per-room listing, its contents, the room filter and the `LIMIT`. **Seen red** against the unfixed module: 7 assertions failed (every listing empty), then 11 of 11 green after the fix. `listdrawers` still has no production caller. |
| M1.7g | S | **Done** — 2026-09-25 | **An access does not extend a memory's life** — Found 2026-09-24 closing T6.3. `core.memory.decay.refresh` writes `accessed_at` on the score row and logs "refreshed memory decay clock", but `sweeprows` ages every row by `created_at` and never reads `accessed_at`, so a memory used every day decays exactly as fast as one never touched. There is also no access count. Decide whether decay should run from the later of `created_at` and `accessed_at` (the natural reading of "refresh") or whether access should raise the stored score; either way `tests/unit/memory/test_decay.tk` has the store fixture to assert it. Not changed in T6.3 because `decay.tk` was being edited on another branch. **Done 2026-09-25.** Decision: decay runs from the LATER of `created_at` and `accessed_at`, the natural reading of "refresh". `decay.sweepsql` takes the MAX of both, each normalised as in M1.7c and COALESCEd so one unreadable value cannot null it. No access count was added, and none is claimed. The fixture's `setscore` now backdates `accessed_at` together with `created_at`, since a backdated memory has not been used since. **Evidence:** in `tests/unit/memory/test_decay.tk`, two memories made at 1600000000, one refreshed a minute before a strict sweep: it survives, and the idle one of the same age is evicted, alone. With the old `decay.tk`, 2 assertions failed. |
| M1.7e | S | **Done** — 2026-09-25 | **A memory export writes each drawer's `created_at` as its year, and `exportrange` compares TEXT with digits** — found 2026-09-24 while closing LS1.1k. `memory_drawers.created_at` is `datetime('now')` TEXT (`storedrawertx`), and `export.tk`'s `rowtojson`, `rowtocsv2` and `buildcontentmd` all read it with `db.rowi64`, which is strtoll: every exported drawer says `2026`. `exportrange` binds `str.fromint(fromts)`/`str.fromint(tots)` and filters `md.created_at >= ? AND md.created_at <= ?`, a lexicographic comparison of `2026-09-24 ...` against ten digits, so the window is meaningless. `importjson` then writes the exported value back as the drawer's `created_at`, so after a round trip the column holds both representations. Fix with the normalising expression `decay.sweepsql` uses (M1.7c) in both export queries, and decide one representation for `memory_drawers.created_at`. **Done 2026-09-25.** One representation, normalised on read: both export queries select, filter and order on the M1.7c expression (epoch seconds from INTEGER text, `strftime('%s')` from datetime TEXT) and bind the window as integers. **Evidence:** in `test_import_json.tk`, a window around 1790000000 exports exactly the three drawers made then; a window around a TEXT-dated drawer finds it as a time; `exportall` writes that drawer as `"created_at":1789862400` and none as a bare year. With the old module those assertions failed. |
| M1.7f | S | **Done** — 2026-09-25 | **An imported memory loses its sensitivity and its PII flag** — found 2026-09-24 while closing LS1.1k. `storedrawer` sets `pii_flagged=1` for CONFIDENTIAL and RESTRICTED drawers so `core.memory.privacy.forgetpii` can find them (M1.7a). The export carries no `sensitivity` field, and `importjson` writes neither `memory_drawers.sensitivity` (so the column defaults to `PUBLIC`) nor `memory_scores.pii_flagged` (defaults to 0). A RESTRICTED memory exported and re-imported is PUBLIC and unflagged, so "forget my personal data" misses it. Add `sensitivity` to the export formats and have the import set both columns. Until then the import should flag rather than trust: an unknown sensitivity is not PUBLIC. **Done 2026-09-25.** The JSON export carries each memory's `sensitivity`. `importitem` writes it to `memory_drawers.sensitivity` and sets `memory_scores.pii_flagged` for CONFIDENTIAL and RESTRICTED. A missing or unrecognised sensitivity, which covers every export written before this, is imported as CONFIDENTIAL and flagged, never as PUBLIC (`importsensitivity`). **Evidence:** 8 assertions in `tests/unit/memory/test_import_json.tk`: a legacy element comes back CONFIDENTIAL and flagged; RESTRICTED comes back RESTRICTED and flagged; PUBLIC stays PUBLIC and unflagged; the export contains the sensitivity. With the old `export.tk`, 4 of them failed. The CSV and Markdown exports do not carry it, because they cannot be imported. |

## Epic M2: AAAK Shorthand Integration

*AAAK is compressed English shorthand readable by any LLM without a decoder — not a programming language, not a binary format. Complementary to TOON (F4.1, structured data) and toke (prompt syntax): AAAK specifically optimises natural-language memory and context loading.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| M1.8 | L | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **The memory palace and the store are two databases, and `std.db` has one connection** — Found 2026-09-23 by a de-stubbed test. `core.storage.db.openstore` opens `datadir/loke.db`; `core.memory.palace.openpalace` opens `datadir/memory.db`. `std.db` keeps a **single global connection** (`g_db`, `src/stdlib/db.c:10`) and `conn_db` resolves only conn_id 0, so `db.open`'s returned handle selects nothing and **every query from both subsystems goes to whichever database was opened last** — filed upstream as T-30. `audit_log` does not exist in `memory.db` and `memory_drawers` does not exist in `loke.db`, so the loser's queries fail **silently**, because `rowi64`/`rowstr` map a failed read to `0`/`""`. It was invisible until `tests/unit/memory/test_palace_drawers.tk` was de-stubbed and started asserting the tables exist. Three routes, and the choice is architectural rather than mechanical: put the memory tables in `loke.db` and drop the second file (simplest, and a single file is one thing to encrypt and one thing to delete — see X8.2/X8.4); keep two files and re-open the right one before every operation (correct but fragile, and it serialises); or wait for T-30 and pass the handle. **Do not "fix" it by reordering the opens** — that makes the working case depend on call order, which is the defect. Sibling of M1.7, which is the four memory tables no migration creates at all **Update 2026-09-24:** the route was chosen and the code landed in `3ead30d` (M1.7). The palace opens `loke.db` and the memory tables come from migrations, so this is route one. `tests/unit/memory/test_palace_one_db.tk` opens the store, then `palace.open` on the same datadir. It asserts that `audit_log` and `settings` are still visible, that `memory_drawers` is in the same database, and that no `memory.db` is created. It passes, 7 of 7 memory tests. A probe confirmed the assertion discriminates: a second `db.open` on `memory.db` after `openstore` makes `tableexists("audit_log")` false.<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-24.** Seen red against the old behaviour: with `palace.open` mutated back to `str.concat(datadir;"/memory.db")` and the tree rebuilt, `tests/unit/memory/test_palace_one_db.tk` failed 3 assertions (`audit_log` not visible after `palace.open`, `settings` not visible, a `memory.db` created). Restored, rebuilt, green. |
| M2.1 | L | **Done** | AAAK encoder/decoder (encoder: natural language → AAAK; e.g. "Priya manages Driftwood: Kai backend 3yr, Soren frontend, Maya infra" → `TEAM: PRI(lead) \| KAI(backend,3yr) SOR(frontend) MAY(infra)`; decoder: AAAK → natural language for human review; 5–30x compression; lossless for structured facts; works with any LLM; configurable compression level; benchmark token savings; integrates with mcp-toke F7.4) |
| M2.2 | L | **Done** | Layered context loading (L0 ~20 tokens: identity; L1 ~150 tokens: AAAK palace map; L2 ~500 tokens: AAAK active context for current task; L3 variable: deep memory via M1.3 semantic search; startup loads L0+L1 only; L2 on task identification; L3 on-demand; configurable layer sizes; total budget enforced; progressive disclosure of what's loaded) |
| M2.3 | M | **Done** | AAAK prompt shorthand (users write shorthand: `refactor auth.module \| extract.jwt.validation → separate.util \| keep.backward.compat`; loke expands locally before pipeline; optional — natural language always works; dots/pipes/arrows syntax; user-defined abbreviations via `loke shorthand add`; expanded version shown in pre-send preview) |
| M2.4 | L | **Done** | Memory mining from external sources (import: Claude exports, ChatGPT exports, Slack JSON, markdown, code repos; mining modes: projects/conversations/general; privacy pipeline scan on import; local SLM processing only; incremental mining; `loke memory mine <path> --mode <type>`; interruptible and resumable; source attribution on mined content) |
| M2.5 | L | **Done** | Memory MCP server (tools: `memory.search`, `memory.store`, `memory.facts`, `memory.context`, `memory.diary_write`, `memory.diary_read`, `memory.status`; all responses pass through privacy pipeline; access control: search allowed, delete/modify requires explicit permission; auto-registered with F7.3 MCP broker; compatible with Claude Code, Codex, and any MCP-capable tool) |

| X4.6 | S | **Done** | **A sweep commit must be provably mechanical** — `scripts/verify_held_changes.py` already checks that held toke changes are an equality-only substitution, it existed when `09b0a67` landed, and it correctly flags a line-count change as *"not a substitution"*. But it guards the **working tree**, so once a change is committed it has nothing to compare against — and nothing checks that a commit *described* as a mechanical sweep actually was one. That gap cost 161 lines of working request-path wiring (GA1.10). Add a pre-push check: for a commit touching more than N files whose message names a mechanical migration, any file with a net line delta beyond a small threshold must be listed explicitly in the message. The signal is cheap and specific — in `09b0a67` exactly one file of 24 shrank by more than 30 lines *Done 2026-09-23. `scripts/check_sweep_commit.py` runs over COMMITS rather than the working tree, which is the gap: `verify_held_changes.py` had already named the exact file and written "it must never be committed from the working tree", and its own docstring says it reports rather than enforces — so once the change was committed there was nothing left to compare against. The rule is not "do not delete" but "say what you deleted": any file losing more than 30 lines net must have its name appear in the commit message, and four PROTECTED files (the request route, the privacy pipeline, the placeholder map, the audit trail) must be named on any net loss of 5. **Proven both ways:** `--range 09b0a67~1..09b0a67 --all-history` reproduces the original failure, and today's 14 commits pass. Also delivered: `githooks/pre-push` plus `scripts/install-hooks.sh` using `core.hooksPath` so the hook is a version-controlled, reviewable artefact — and the gate now asserts `core.hooksPath` is actually set, because X4.2 was marked Done claiming a pre-push hook while `.git/hooks` held only `.sample` files and nothing in the tree could have revealed it. pre-PUSH not pre-COMMIT, deliberately: a 10-second pre-commit hook gets `--no-verify`d within a day, and a hook people bypass is worse than none because it creates the impression of a control.* |
| X4.7 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **Audit the nine unexplained deletions that predate the X4.6 cutover** — `check_sweep_commit.py` applies from `531708a` forward, because commits before it cannot be amended without rewriting history that may already have been fetched. All look like legitimate work whose messages simply did not enumerate files — but `09b0a67` looked like that too, and it had removed response restoration, policy evaluation and audit logging. Two of these touch files already known to be defective: `governance/report.tk` has a function with no return statement at all (fixed 2026-09-23) and `privacy/test_harness.tk` had three latent defects. Read each diff and record, per deletion, whether anything working was lost. If something was, recover it as GA1.10 did **Done 2026-09-24. One of the nine lost something working, and it is recovered.** Method: for each commit and file, list every function definition and import that was removed and not re-added in the same commit, then read what remained. **`4894533` pipeline.tk (−43)** and **`4d6e0ec`** (report.tk −60, queue.tk −42, pipeline.tk −10, test_harness.tk −37): no definition or import was lost. Each deletion was a body rewritten in place. **`d14e0bd` test files** (−98/−67/−60/−36, in test_rules_engine, test_tiers, test_backup and test_ephemeral): these removed local RE-DECLARATIONS of the module under test (`evaluate`/`evalcondition`, `tierlabel`/`configfortier`, eight backup functions, the six-function ephemeral design that never existed) and replaced them with imports of the real module. That is the X7.2 de-stubbing, and the gain is real. **`d14e0bd` also dropped four imports from `browser/pages/api/pipeline.tk`**: placeholder, policy, audit and keychain. GA1.10 later restored the first three and **not keychain**. So since d14e0bd the request path read provider keys only from `settings.json`, while `/api/settings` saves a key to the keychain and removes it from `settings.json`. **A key saved through the settings screen was never found, and every cloud route was refused as "no anthropic_api_key is configured."** Recovered: `resolvekey` prefers the keychain and falls back to `settings.json`, the same order `settings.keyfordisplay` uses, and it normalises the NULL that `json.str` returns for an absent field. The keychain is read only once a cloud route has been chosen. Four assertions in `tests/unit/browser/test_pipeline_handler.tk`; with the keychain branch disabled, two of them failed. The real keychain call is not covered, for the reason `core.storage.keychain` states: a headless test cannot answer its authorisation dialog. |

| X4.9 | S | **Done** | **A stale test binary in `/tmp/loke-tests` produces phantom failures, twice in one session** — `scripts/run_tests.sh` rebuilds `libloke.a` when any object is newer than the archive, but it does **not** compare each per-test binary against its source or the archive. So a suite run that overlaps an edit leaves `/tmp/loke-tests/<name>` built against a half-written tree, and every later run re-uses it. Observed twice on 2026-09-24, both times as a test failing in a full run while passing in isolation and on a fresh database — `test_pipeline_api` once, `test_kill_switch` once — and both times cleared by `rm -rf /tmp/loke-tests` with no source change. That is the worst shape a build-cache bug can take here, because the obvious reading is an order-dependent product defect and the session spent real time chasing one. Two candidate fixes: stat each test binary against its `.tk` and the archives and rebuild when older (cheap, and matches what the archive step already does), or make `BUILDDIR` default to a per-run directory now that it is overridable. Prefer the first — a per-run directory throws away the caching the script exists to provide. Until then, `rm -rf /tmp/loke-tests` before trusting a surprising failure. **Done 2026-09-24.** `scripts/run_tests.sh` refuses to run when a `.tk` source is newer than the newest object in the directory the tests link against, naming the object, up to three newer sources, and the build command to run. It had already produced a false product defect **three times** — `test_pipeline_api`, `test_kill_switch`, and `test_pipeline_api` again while GA5.10 was being verified — each time costing a careful reading of a module that was not wrong. Once in the other direction too: a test passed against stale objects after its subject had changed. The existing freshness check compared the ARCHIVE against the OBJECTS, which is the wrong end of the chain; nothing compared the objects against the source. It **refuses rather than rebuilding** deliberately — rebuilding would make this script a second build system with its own opinion about module order and which trees exist, and when that opinion drifts from `build_loke.sh` the symptom is an unplaceable linker error. Proved by touching a source and observing exit 2 with the file named. Separately: **never run two suites concurrently** — both share `/tmp/loke-tests`, and doing so produced a run reporting 290 of 290, each test counted twice. |
| X4.10 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **Every ratchet baseline was gitignored, so no ratchet could fail outside this working copy** — found while adding the lint ratchet, which would have inherited the same defect. `build_census.py` wrote its floor to `build/trees/<tree>/census-baseline.json`, and `build/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:7`). So the baselines were **never committed**: on a fresh clone, and therefore in CI, there was no floor at all, `--check` had nothing to compare against, and the per-tree ratchet F10.23b exists to provide was inert everywhere except the machine that recorded it. That is the **fifth** check in this repository found unable to fail for a reason of this kind, after `ci.yml`'s `grep … \|\| echo` licence check, `verify_held_changes.py` reporting rather than enforcing, `find_sql_literal_now.py`'s length-deleting comment stripper, and the `--emit-llvm` flag whose absence made 146 of 179 interfaces silently missing. Fixed by moving every floor to a committed `baselines/` directory — `census-<tree>.json` and `lint.json` — with a README that states the rule the files exist to enforce: **re-record a baseline only downwards**, because moving the floor up to make a run green is the one use of them that defeats their purpose. Verified: the census still reads its floor (`baseline 0 -> now 0`), and the lint ratchet fires on a forced regression (baseline lowered by 50 → exit 1). |
| X4.11 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **T-27 was documented for weeks with nothing detecting it, and it cost two build cycles in ten minutes** — comments NEST in toke, so one stray open-paren-star inside a comment opens another level and a single close closes only one: the rest of the file is swallowed. The module still **type-checks**, because the swallowed text simply is not there; what is lost is every declaration after the comment, and it surfaces somewhere else entirely as `module X has no exported member Y`. Both failures were mine, on 2026-09-24, and the second is the instructive one. (1) A comment added to `core/storage/db.tk` contained a SQL aggregate written with a star: `db.tk` checked clean, its interface silently lost every export after that comment, and **fourteen call sites across six modules** failed — none of them near the cause. (2) The paragraph written to explain (1) contained the sequence **twice**, in prose, while describing it. `scripts/find_nested_comment_open.py` is now in the gate over `packages` and `src`. It reports a file that ENDS at comment depth greater than zero, which is exact rather than heuristic — a source file whose comment never closes is wrong in every circumstance, so there is no allowlist and no judgement. Its first version flagged nested depth alone and produced **three false positives**, all comments written earlier the same day that quote defective code which itself contains a comment: balanced nesting is legal, harmless, and must not be flagged. Verified both ways — clean across 454 files, exit 1 on a reintroduced hazard. |
| X4.12 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **Interpolating a `std.str` result printed a pointer, and the privacy pipeline's summary did it three times** — Found 2026-09-24 while clearing a lint ratchet. Interpolating `str.fromi64(n)` renders the string's address, because `std.str` declares the function's return as `i64` (T-41 upstream, T-36 family). Measured one name at a time: 15 functions are affected, and `tolower`/`toupper`/`substr` are not, so the set cannot be derived from the interface. **Two live sites.** `core.privacy.pipeline.summariserun` printed addresses for the total, RESTRICTED, CONFIDENTIAL and stage counts. Its test checked only for the words, so it passed. `moke.workspace.newproject` gave every project the id `p-<address>`. Both are fixed: the bindings are annotated `:str` in one and `str.concat` is used in the other. Two assertions now pin the counts in `test_pipeline_orchestrator.tk`, and both FAILED against the unfixed module before they were trusted. `scripts/find_interp_abi_str.py` is wired into `quality-gate.sh --step checks`. It flags direct interpolation and unannotated bindings of the 15 names, and reports 0 sites across `packages` and `tests` |

## Epic X4a: Module-Aware API Handler Compilation

*loke's API handlers (`pages/api/*.tk`) are toke source files that import modules from the core engine (`core.privacy.pipeline`, `core.router`, `shared.types`, etc.). Currently ooke compiles page templates but falls through to a static echo for API handlers because the toke compiler cannot resolve cross-module imports at serve time. This means all API endpoints (`/api/pipeline`, `/api/health`, `/api/models`, etc.) return either ooke's default response or echo the request body — no loke business logic executes.*

**Root cause:** When ooke serves an API route backed by a `.tk` handler, it needs to compile that handler into a callable function. The handler imports other modules via `i=alias:module.path;`, but the compiler has no module search path configured — it can only compile single-file programs. The same gap blocks the test suite (X4b.3).

**Impact:** loke and moke build and serve static pages correctly, but every API call that requires the privacy pipeline, router, model registry, governance gateway, or any core module returns a no-op response. The product is visually complete but functionally inert.

**Dependency:** This requires changes in both toke (module search path, multi-file compilation) and ooke (passing module roots to the compiler when compiling handlers). The `--emit-deps` standalone build pipeline works for single-file programs (proven by the test suite) but not for files with cross-module imports.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| X4a.1 | XL | **Done** | **toke: multi-file module resolution** — Add a `--module-path <dir>` flag (repeatable) to toke that tells the compiler where to search for imported modules. When compiling `source.tk` that contains `i=alias:module.path;`, the compiler searches each `--module-path` directory for a file whose `m=` declaration matches `module.path`. Resolves the module's type interface (generates `.tki` if needed) so the importing file can type-check. At link time, compiles all transitively imported modules and links them together. This is the critical missing piece — without it, no multi-file toke program can compile. |
| X4a.2 | L | **Done** | **ooke: pass module paths when compiling handlers** — When ooke compiles an API handler `.tk` file, pass the project's source directories as `--module-path` arguments to toke. For loke, this means: `--module-path packages/core/src --module-path packages/shared/src --module-path packages/browser/src --module-path src`. Read module paths from `ooke.toml` config (new `[build] module_paths = [...]` field). Handlers should compile with full access to the project's module graph. |
| X4a.3 | L | **Done** | **Verify loke API handlers compile and execute** — Once X4a.1 and X4a.2 are complete, verify that all loke API handlers compile with module resolution and return correct responses: `/api/health` (returns loke version, Ollama status, port), `/api/pipeline` (runs privacy pipeline on input text, returns anonymised output), `/api/models` (returns available model list from Ollama), `/api/settings` (returns current config). Test from moke's chat interface — a prompt should flow through the full pipeline and return an LLM response. **Re-verified 2026-09-24 (RV1.2): this was FALSE until today, and it is true now.** The handlers compiled, but with the server running, `/api/health`, `/api/models` and `/api/pipeline` each killed the worker process on their first request. The pages `/privacy`, `/savings` and `/pipeline` did the same. The cause was F10.36: ooke objects compiled by an older toke clobbered the runtime's thread-local error flag. Fixed there. Evidence now: `scripts/smoke_serve.sh` makes a real request to every route and fails on a dropped connection. It is in `quality-gate.sh`'s test step, and it went red against the old objects before it was trusted. |
| X4a.4 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-25 | **Verify moke API handlers compile and execute** — Verify moke's API handlers: `/api/health` (proxies to loke health), `/api/feedback` (stores feedback), `/api/memory` (searches memory palace), `/api/datasets` (lists uploaded datasets), `/api/upload` (processes CSV upload), `/api/pipeline` (proxies pipeline call through loke). **Reopened 2026-09-24 (RV1.2).** The moke tree has 0 compile errors, but six objects still fail IR assembly (MK21.16), so there is no moke binary. A handler cannot "execute and return correct responses" from a server that cannot be built. This row is also the cited evidence for retiring BUG-7, so that retirement no longer has evidence either. The BUG-7 line in the BG1 migration table is annotated to match. **Done 2026-09-25, re-verified against the running server.** moke links (MK21.16). `scripts/smoke_serve_moke.sh` requests 11 pages and loads all 17 datasets through `/api/datasets`, and it is in the gate. Running it the first time fixed `/` and `/chat`, which returned null (LS1.1u). |

## Epic X4a.5: Core Module Semantic Fixes

*With toke v3 syntax migration complete (562/562 files) and module-aware compilation working (X4a.1–X4a.2), 94 of 168 core modules still fail semantic checks. These are code-level issues — undeclared identifiers, missing imports, type mismatches, and mutability errors — that prevent `.tki` interface generation and block the full module dependency graph from resolving.*

**Root cause:** The v3 migration fixed syntax but not semantics. Many modules reference sum type variants, stdlib functions, or types without proper imports or declarations. Some use `i64` where `i32`/`u32` is expected. Some assign to immutable bindings.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| X4a.5.1 | S | **Done** | **Add missing `std.json` imports** — 9 modules call `json.str()`, `json.i64()`, etc. without `i=json:std.json;`. Add the import to: `core.feedback.store`, `core.governance.aigov`, `core.governance.consent`, `core.governance.dsar`, `core.governance.monitoring`, `core.governance.quota`, `core.governance.report`, `core.governance.rulesengine`, `core.storage.syncqueue`. |
| X4a.5.2 | S | **Done** | **Fix `let` → `let mut` bindings** — 12 modules assign to immutable bindings. Add `mut.` prefix: `core.companion.discovery` (n), `core.eval.advisor` (runline), `core.governance.compliance` (result), `core.governance.scorecard` (rows), `core.governance.value` (result), `core.installer.uninstall` (models), `core.installer.updater` (found), `core.mcp.protocol` (items), `core.policy.loader` (sets), `core.privacy.placeholderstore` (result), `core.privacy.presidio` (entities), `core.storage.dashboard` (found). |
| X4a.5.3 | M | **Done** | **Fix type mismatches (`i32`/`u32` vs `i64`)** — 11 modules pass `i64` where `i32` or `u32` is expected (or vice versa). Add explicit casts (`as i32`, `as u32`) in: `core.agents.observability`, `core.governance.forecast`, `core.governance.killswitch`, `core.memory.export`, `core.memory.privacy`, `core.optimiser.llmlingua`, `core.router.intent`, `core.router.latencyrouter`, `core.router.sensitivity`, `core.storage.ephemeral`. Fix `void` return mismatches in `core.governance.policy`, `core.memory.aaak`. |
| X4a.5.4 | M | **Done** | **Add missing imports and type declarations** — Fix undeclared identifiers by adding imports or local type definitions. Missing imports: `core.mcp.discovery` (std.time), `core.memory.decay` (std.math), `core.auth.oauth` (std.crypto), `core.auth.refresh` (std.http), `core.feedback.reporter` (shared.types), `core.pipeline.history` (shared.types). Missing type declarations: `core.models.hardware` ($hwvendor), `core.models.infer` ($inferbackend), `core.models.mlx` ($mlxloaded), `core.models.queue` ($queuedrequest), `core.optimiser.budget` ($budgetperiod), `core.optimiser.toon` ($toontoken), `core.privacy.template` ($templateerr), `core.memory.schema` ($dbconn import from core.storage.db). |
| X4a.5.5 | M | **Done** | **Fix wrong import paths (remaining)** — Modules with incorrect import paths: `core.companion.executor` (companion.channel → core.companion.channel), `core.eval.bench` (providers.dispatcher → core.providers.dispatcher), `core.memory.mining` (memory.aaak → core.memory.aaak). |
| X4a.5.6 | S | **Done** | **Fix duplicate identifier declarations** — 3 modules declare the same identifier twice: `core.agents.sandbox` (readfile), `core.governance.usecases` (usecase), `shared.result` ($ok conflicts with built-in). Rename duplicates or remove redundant declarations. |
| X4a.5.7 | S | **Done** | **Fix `mut` parse errors** — 2 modules have `mut` in expression position where it's not valid: `core.extensions.providerregistry`, `core.installer.pull`. Likely double-mutation from prior automated fix — clean up. |
| X4a.5.8 | S | **Done** | **Suppress warnings / minor fixes** — Handle: `core.agents.overnight` (Python keyword `class`), `core.memory.search` / `core.router.examples` / `loke.browser.workspace.tabs` (value escapes scope — move binding). These are warnings, not errors, but should be cleaned up. |

## Epic X4a.6: Live API Handler Implementations

*loke builds and serves a native binary (X4a.1–X4a.5 complete). **Corrected 2026-09-20: this was false** — 58 of 199 modules compiled and the suite had not run. **Re-corrected 2026-09-23: the build is now green (218 of 218, 0 errors) and the suite runs 122 of 122.** So the original claim is true of the build and still false of the handlers, which is the point of this epic. The claim was written while the interface directory held a stale nested tree that toke never read, so modules were type-checked against four-month-old signatures. See F10 and RV1. But the API handlers return stub/placeholder data because the core module functions (`browser.extensions.core`, `core.models.ollama`) have placeholder implementations. This epic implements the real business logic so API endpoints return live data.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| X4a.6.1 | M | **Done** | **Implement `browser.extensions.core` state management** — Replace stub getters (`getconfig`, `getrouter`, `getdispatcher`, etc.) with real state. On init: load `ooke.toml` config, create Ollama client, initialise privacy pipeline config with default entity types and detection layers. Store state in a module-level struct initialised once at startup. `getconfig()` returns a struct with `.ollamaurl` (default `http://127.0.0.1:11434`), `.port` (11430), `.datadir`, `.loglevel`. |
| X4a.6.2 | M | **Done** | **Implement `/api/health` — live Ollama check** — The health handler calls `ollama.healthcheck(client)` which should HTTP GET `http://127.0.0.1:11434/api/tags`. Return `{"ok":true,"loke":"0.2.0","ollama":"running"/"stopped","port":11430}`. Use `std.http` GET with timeout. **Re-verified 2026-09-24 (RV1.2), against the running server.** With Ollama not running, `/api/health` answered `{"ok":true,"loke":"0.3.0","ollama":"offline",...}`. That is the honest answer, and it comes from `shared.reachability` rather than a string-matched `http.get`. The online case was not exercised, because no Ollama was available. |
| X4a.6.3 | M | **Done** | **Implement `/api/models` — list from Ollama** — GET `http://127.0.0.1:11434/api/tags`, parse JSON response, return model list with name/size. Use `std.http` and `std.json`. **Re-verified 2026-09-24 (RV1.2).** With Ollama offline, `/api/models` answered `{"models":[]}` with 200. The online listing was not exercised. |
| X4a.6.4 | L | **Done** | **Implement `/api/pipeline` — proxy to Ollama** — Accept `{text, system_prompt, model}`, POST to `http://127.0.0.1:11434/api/generate` with model and prompt, return response. Privacy pipeline runs first (placeholder pass-through for now). **Re-verified 2026-09-24 (RV1.2).** The row's design, a plain proxy to Ollama, is superseded: `/api/pipeline` now runs the privacy pipeline and the governance gates before any provider. It answers over HTTP, with a 502 "provider request failed" when no provider is reachable, which is correct. The forwarding path is asserted by `tests/integration/test_pipeline_api.tk`. |
| X4a.6.5 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-25 | **Wire moke → loke pipeline** — moke's chat sends to loke's `/api/pipeline`, loke proxies to Ollama, response flows back. Verify end-to-end from moke UI. **Reopened 2026-09-24 (RV1.2).** "Verify end-to-end from moke UI" cannot have been done in the current tree: moke does not link (MK21.16). **Done 2026-09-25: the first time this path has ever run.** `scripts/smoke_e2e.sh` runs a recording fake Ollama, loke and moke, and POSTs a prompt containing a synthetic email to moke's `/api/pipeline`. It asserts that the request succeeds end to end, that the model received only a placeholder (`[EMAIL_2e31_1]` on the run recorded), and that the user got the email back, restored by loke. With loke changed to send `res.original` to the model, it FAILED on the leak and on the restoration, which proves the leak check is live. It is in the gate's test step. Four defects stood in the way and are recorded separately: MK21.19, LS1.1v, F2.11 and T-48. |

## Epic X4a.7: CORS Architecture Resolution

*Cross-origin issues between moke (:11432) and loke (:11430) have been a recurring problem. The `ooke.toml` `corsorigins = "*"` setting works for preflight (OPTIONS) and actual requests when the Origin header is present, but the browser-side JS health polling still shows connectivity issues. This epic proposes an architectural change to eliminate CORS as a concern.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| X4a.7.1 | S | **Done** | **Audit current CORS state** — Document exactly where CORS is configured (ooke.toml, ooke http.c headers), what the browser sees (inspect Network tab), and whether the issue is CORS headers missing on specific routes or a different connectivity problem (e.g. moke's JS fetching wrong URL, health endpoint returning non-JSON). |
| X4a.7.2 | M | **Done** | **Auto-detect same-origin or cross-origin** — `base.tkt` probes `/api/health` as a relative path first. If it returns a valid loke response (has `d.loke` field), sets `window.LOKE_URL = ''` so all templates use relative paths (no CORS). If the probe fails, falls back to `CROSS_ORIGIN_URL = 'http://127.0.0.1:11430'` for dev-mode cross-origin. All templates already use `window.LOKE_URL \|\| 'http://127.0.0.1:11430'` fallback. |
| X4a.7.3 | S | **Done** | **Both approaches work** — Same-origin works when loke serves moke (single port or reverse proxy). Cross-origin works in dev (moke:11432 → loke:11430) with ooke CORS headers. No code changes needed per deployment — auto-detection handles it. |
| X4a.7.4 | S | **Done** | **Verified implementation** — `probeAndResolve()` in base.tkt handles the decision at runtime. `corsorigins` kept in ooke.toml as a safety net for dev mode. Templates use `window.LOKE_URL + '/api/...'` which resolves to either relative or absolute paths. |

## Epic X4b: Archive Legacy Tests & Create New Test Suites

*The v3 migration converted all 562 source files to valid toke v3 syntax. The 170 existing test files (156 loke + 14 moke) were written against the pre-ooke architecture using patterns that no longer compile (v2 test framework references, unresolvable module imports, pre-migration assertion patterns). These tests need to be archived and replaced with clean test suites for loke and moke separately.*

**Blocker:** The toke compiler currently links `tk_web_glue.o` on every build, which requires ooke's db/collections C libraries. A standalone `--no-web-glue` compilation mode is needed in toke before any test binary can be produced. This is being addressed in the toke project. Once resolved, test files can compile to standalone binaries via `tkc --out <binary> <test.tk>`.

**Context:** `std.test` exists in toke's stdlib (`tk_test.c` / `tk_test.h`) and provides `test.assert(cond;msg)`, `test.assert_eq(a;b;msg)`, `test.assert_ne(a;b;msg)`. Test files should follow the convention `f=main():i64` returning 0 for all-pass, non-zero for failure count. The existing `.tkc.md` companion files (156 of them) document the intended test behaviour and can be used as specifications for rewriting.

### Part 1: Archive

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| X4b.1 | S | **Done** | **Archive legacy loke test files** — Move the 156 existing test files from `tests/unit/`, `tests/integration/`, and `tests/e2e/` into `_archived-tests/loke/`. Preserve directory structure. These files are syntactically valid v3 but cannot compile due to unresolvable module imports and broken test framework references. Keep the `.tkc.md` companion files alongside their test files — they document intended test behaviour and serve as specs for rewriting. Add a `_archived-tests/README.md` explaining the archive reason (v3 migration, test framework gap) and that companion files contain the canonical test specifications. |
| X4b.2 | S | **Done** | **Archive legacy moke test files** — Move the 14 existing test files from `packages/moke/tests/` into `_archived-tests/moke/`. Same approach: preserve structure, keep companion files, document the archive. The 3 test files we rewrote during migration (`feedback_test.tk`, `memory_test.tk`, `pipeline_panel_test.tk`) are placeholder stubs — archive them with the rest. |

*A clean test suite for the loke core engine. Each test file is a standalone `.tk` program that imports only `std.test` and the module under test. Tests are grouped by subsystem. Every test file compiles independently and returns 0 on success.*

It is now case-insensitive, and so is `severityrank`. Both assertions failed against the old module. The test also covers `evaluate` matching one rule, none and two. It is now six digits from 32 random bits; a constant code fails the test. `savecompanion` matched a user-sum result and returned true whatever happened (T-18); it now uses `std.db` with bools. `confirm` and `reject` are added. `tests/unit/companion/test_exo.tk` has 14 assertions: a dead Exo's health check and cluster are false and empty, and a cluster body yields its nodes, each node's model list, memory, total and summary. **Defects:** the total was 9.3e18 for 192 + 64 GB, because `json.getf32` does not answer a usable f32, so memory is read through typed `json.f64`. A malformed body crashed the untyped accessors (LS1.1w), so a shape check now runs first. Zeroing the memory read fails 2 assertions. The port probe added before the health check is defensive and was not caught by mutation: `http.get(url)` on a closed port returned an error here rather than a nil `$ok`. `tests/unit/cli/test_init.tk` has 9 assertions: profile path per shell, the snippet, the decision whether a profile needs it (so init is safe twice; mutation fails 2), and a closed-port Ollama check. It also spliced the session name into its SQL. Fixed with its own `cli_sessions` table (migration **33**), bound parameters, and bools. `switchto` refuses an unknown name before deactivating the current session, and `new` reports a failed creation. The old module crashes the new test. So the Done now stands on all five suites exercising production code. |

Worse, `json.dec` accepts `{not json` and reading its result crashes (T-51), so the decoder's verdict could not be the guard. **Fixed:** `wellformed` checks bytes with `str.charat` (a single `{...}`, braces and brackets balanced outside strings, nothing after it), then `isrequest` requires a decodable object with a `method`. Anything else is answered `-32600 Invalid Request`. With the old `server.tk`, the test crashed. **Also found:** the moke tree compiles its own copy of mcp-toke, so a test linked moke's STALE mcptoke objects and the fix was not in the binary. `run_tests.sh`'s stale-build guard now checks every root the moke tree compiles. |

# WRAPPER PROJECT SCOPE

*The following requirements from the platform requirements document are domain-specific or application-level concerns. They should be implemented in the application built on loke, not in loke itself. loke provides the extension points; the wrapper provides the implementation.*

| Requirement | Why it's wrapper scope | loke provides |
|---|---|---|
| **R8.3 — Calendar integration** (Microsoft Graph adapter, list/create/update/delete events, free/busy) | Domain-specific — not every loke application needs calendar. | P5.1 integration adapter interface + P5.2 OAuth 2.0 flow. Wrapper implements the calendar adapter. |
| **R8.4 — Conferencing integration** (Teams/Zoom meeting links, recordings, transcripts) | Domain-specific. | P5.1 adapter interface. Wrapper implements conferencing adapters. |
| **R9.2 — Multi-timezone display** (simultaneous timezone comparison) | Meeting-planning UX, not core to privacy/LLM proxy. | P4.3 locale-aware date/time formatting. Wrapper builds the multi-tz UI. |
| **R9.3 — Meeting planner** (timezone overlap grid, working hours, participant availability) | Productivity-app feature. | UTC storage (F6.1), Intl APIs (P4.3). Wrapper builds the planner. |
| **R9.4 — InterPlanet timezone provider** | Novelty/future-proof feature, not core. | P4.3 uses standard Intl.DateTimeFormat. Wrapper implements custom provider. |
| **R10.1–R10.3 — P2P metadata sync, sync status, conflict resolution** | Multi-user collaboration beyond loke's local-first single-user model. F8 companion devices cover compute offload, not data sync. | F8 companion device infrastructure (mDNS, TLS). Wrapper builds sync protocol and conflict resolution on top. |
| **R11.3 — Central notifications** (policy changes, announcements from central source) | Enterprise distribution concern. | P3.8 notification system. Wrapper pushes notifications from its central endpoint. |
| **R11.4 — Central authentication** (each install authenticates against configurable endpoint) | Enterprise deployment concern. | P1.3 middleware pipeline with pluggable auth. Wrapper implements the auth adapter. |
| **R14.3 — Enhancement request form** (structured feature request with use case, priority) | Application-level UX, not platform infrastructure. | A5.1 issue reporting form pattern. Wrapper extends with enhancement-specific fields. |

### Timezone note

loke stores all timestamps in UTC (F6.1) and uses `Intl` APIs for display formatting (P4.3). R9.1 (UTC throughout) and R9.5 (relative time) are covered by P4.3. The wrapper is responsible for any multi-timezone comparison UI, meeting planning, or custom timezone providers.

### Multi-user note

loke is local-first and single-user by design. The companion device support (F8) provides secure device-to-device communication for compute offload. If the wrapper needs multi-user data sync, it should build on F8's mDNS discovery and TLS channel, implementing its own sync protocol, conflict resolution, and metadata scope. loke's F6.6 sync queue provides the persistent outbound queue primitive.

---

# DEMO LAYER — moke

*moke is a data analysis demo application built on loke, demonstrating the extension API (P2.6–P2.8) and showing what a domain-specific application looks like. moke is a toke/ooke package at `/packages/moke/` that imports from `core.*` and adds its own pages, data, and pipeline stages without forking any loke source. It is the reference implementation of loke's extensibility pattern.*

*The original moke was a Node.js/Express prototype (`/moke/`) that proved the schema-first data protocol, local compute engine, dashboard generation, and Insight Lab concepts. The toke moke re-implements all DONE features from that prototype as a first-class ooke application.*

---

## Epic MK1: Core Data Analysis App

*The base moke experience: load a sensitive dataset, have loke anonymise it schema-first (no raw rows to the LLM), ask questions, see answers.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK1.1 | L | **Done** | ooke package scaffold (`packages/moke/ooke.toml`, extension init hook, registers with loke gateway; `moke` command entry point; browser mode on port 11432 alongside loke on 11430) |
| MK1.2 | M | **Done** | Dataset loader (CSV, Excel .xlsx, paste CSV/TSV; PapaParse-equivalent in toke via `std.csv`; first-5-row preview before commit; drag-drop upload handler; stores in session — never persisted) |
| MK1.3 | L | **Done** | Schema-first data protocol hook (P2.6 hook registered `before_anonymise`; intercepts requests containing raw tabular data; extracts column names, types, stats (mean/min/max/nulls/sample 3 values); replaces raw data with TOON-compressed schema profile; raw rows stay local; hook enforced via P2.8 governance rule — blocks any prompt where raw row data detected) |
| MK1.4 | L | **Done** | Local compute engine (15 operations: count, sum, avg, min, max, group, timeseries, topN, distribution, correlate, percentile, countBy, latest, distinct, join; LLM requests computations via structured JSON query; moke executes locally against in-memory dataset; results fed back to LLM as next turn; no raw data ever sent) |
| MK1.5 | M | **Done** | Data profiler (auto-detect column types: numeric/categorical/datetime/boolean/text; compute per-column stats; identify cardinality; detect PII columns by name pattern; produce compact TOON profile; shown in dataset sidebar) |
| MK1.6 | M | **Done** | Sensitivity classification UI (PUBLIC/INTERNAL/CONFIDENTIAL/RESTRICTED selector on dataset load; classification propagates to loke governance gateway as `sensitivity` field in `$eval_context`; colour-coded badge on all dataset views; RESTRICTED forces local-only routing via P2.7 custom provider) |
| MK1.7 | M | **Done** | Pipeline console (real-time stage-by-stage log: schema extraction → profile → query dispatch → local compute → result injection → LLM call → response; colour-coded stages; expandable detail per stage; matches A4.2 panel style) |
| MK1.8 | M | **Done** | Confirmation modal (human-in-the-loop before every LLM call; shows: schema profile being sent vs original data stayed local; PII summary from loke pipeline; entity count; approve/edit/cancel; "don't ask again for this session") |

## Epic MK4.7: Privacy Pipeline Visibility & Governance Demo

*loke's core value proposition — privacy, governance, and transparency — is invisible in the current moke demo. Users see text responses but not what loke did to protect them. This epic makes the pipeline tangible: PII detection counts, anonymisation evidence, sensitivity classification, routing decisions, cost awareness, and human-in-the-loop confirmation.*

**Why this matters:** Without visible privacy features, moke is just another chat-with-data UI. With them, it demonstrates what makes loke unique — the user sees their data being protected in real time.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK4.7.1 | L | **Done** | **Privacy detection display in pipeline trace** — When loke returns a response, show PII detection results in the pipeline trace: entity count by type (names, emails, phones, etc.), which privacy layers detected them (regex, NER, SLM), confidence scores. Requires loke's `/api/pipeline` response to include `entities_found`, `entity_types`, `sensitivity`, `detection_layers` fields. Update pipeline handler to populate these from the privacy filter run. Display in the collapsible pipeline trace panel with colour-coded entity badges. |
| MK4.7.2 | M | **Done** | **Sensitivity classification badge and routing explanation** — Prominent sensitivity badge (PUBLIC green / INTERNAL amber / CONFIDENTIAL orange / RESTRICTED red) on every response. Below the badge, one-line routing explanation: "CONFIDENTIAL → routed to local Ollama (data stays on device)" or "PUBLIC → routed to Claude Sonnet 4 (fastest, $0.003)". Show "Could run locally?" advisor when cloud model was used. |
| MK4.7.3 | L | **Done** | **Human-in-the-loop confirmation modal** — Before each LLM call, show the confirmation modal (confirm.tkt already exists). Display: schema profile being sent (not raw data), PII summary from pipeline, entity count, sensitivity classification, target model/provider, estimated cost. Buttons: Approve / Edit prompt / Use local model / Cancel. Configurable: can be disabled in settings for trusted workflows. |
| MK4.7.4 | M | **Done** | **Real pipeline stages in console** — Replace simulated `setTimeout` stages with actual pipeline timing from loke's response. Show real stages: Schema Extract → Privacy Filter (N entities, Xms) → Token Optimise (Y% saved) → Route (model, reason) → LLM Call (provider, Xms) → Restore (placeholders replaced). Each stage shows duration and outcome. Failed stages show red with error detail. |
| MK4.7.5 | M | **Done** | **Cost and token tracking** — Show token counts (in/out) and cost per request in the pipeline trace. Running session totals in the sidebar: "Session: 12 requests · 4,230 tokens · $0.0127". Compare local vs cloud cost: "This request: $0.003 (Claude) — would be free locally". Monthly savings estimate visible in governance dashboard. |
| MK4.7.6 | S | **Done** | **Anonymisation evidence view** — In the detail/debug panel, show before/after: "Original: john.smith@example.com → Sent: [EMAIL_1]". Show each entity with its placeholder. For demo: use the dataset's PII columns (patient_name, medicare_number etc.) to demonstrate real anonymisation. Never show the mapping in the audit trail — only in the user's local session. |
| MK4.7.7 | S | **Done** | **Feedback capture** — Thumbs up/down on every response stores to localStorage: `{timestamp, requestId, vote, comment, model, provider}`. Thumbs-down opens lightweight comment box. Show feedback count in settings. Export as JSON for development review. |

## Epic MK4.8: Dashboard & Insight Lab End-to-End

*The Dashboard tab and Insight Lab need to work as complete demo flows — from dataset selection through LLM-guided design to rendered interactive output.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK4.8.1 | M | **Done** | **Dashboard tab Phase 1-2-3 pipeline working** — Dashboard tab's generate flow: Phase 1 (LLM designs dashboard via loke pipeline — handle `anthropic_raw` response format), Phase 2 (local compute resolves queries against loaded dataset rows), Phase 3 (render Chart.js charts). Fix the `extractJSON` → `resolveQueries` → render pipeline end-to-end. Test with each built-in dataset. |
| MK4.8.2 | M | **Done** | **Dashboard demo prompts per dataset** — On the Dashboard tab, show dataset-specific dashboard suggestions. Medicare: "Claims by specialty and month", "Cost distribution by state". Water Quality: "pH trends by location", "Alert frequency heatmap". Clicking a suggestion auto-fills and generates. |
| MK4.8.3 | L | **Done** | **Insight Lab meaningful results** — Replace generic "Cluster 1/2" labels with LLM-generated descriptions. Flow: (1) Auto-profile dataset columns, (2) Send schema to LLM asking "propose 3 analyses", (3) Run local ML (k-means, z-score, correlation), (4) Send results back to LLM for narrative interpretation, (5) Display with human-readable labels and visualisations. Each cluster shows: label, size, key characteristics, representative rows. |
| MK4.8.4 | S | **Done** | **Insight Lab demo configs per dataset** — Pre-configured analysis settings per dataset: Customer Intelligence → k=4 clustering on spend/frequency, Medicare → anomaly detection on benefit_paid, Water Quality → correlation matrix on chemical readings. One-click to run with sensible defaults. |
| MK4.8.5 | M | **Done** | **Dashboard export** — Export rendered dashboard as PNG (html2canvas already loaded), JSON DDL (the chart definition), or shareable link (encode DDL in URL hash). Export button on dashboard header. |
| MK4.8.6 | S | **Done** | **Dashboard templates** — Save/load dashboard configurations. Store DDL in localStorage with name. Template selector dropdown on dashboard page. Share templates between datasets. |
| MK4.8.7 | M | **Done** | **Semantic colour coding for chart values** — Dashboard charts should colour-code data values by semantic meaning when grouping by categorical columns. Three tiers: (1) **Known-value matching** — pattern-match group labels against a built-in dictionary: negative/warning/error terms (failed, critical, expired, breach, overdue, rejected, blocked, offline, down, high-risk, P1, RESTRICTED) → red; positive/success terms (passed, healthy, active, approved, resolved, online, up, compliant, low-risk, PUBLIC) → green; neutral/missing terms (unknown, null, n/a, other, pending, none, unclassified) → grey. (2) **LLM-assisted** — when the LLM generates DDL, it can nominate `"colour": "red"/"green"/"amber"/"grey"` per data series based on the question context (e.g., "show servers with expired warranties" → expired = red, valid = green). Add `colour` as an optional field in the DDL card schema. (3) **Local pattern fallback** — for values not in the dictionary and no LLM hint, run a simple client-side classifier: numeric ranges (high = amber/red, low = green for metrics like CPU/error rate; inverted for metrics like uptime/availability), boolean (true = green, false = red), status-like strings (regex against common status patterns). Implement the colour map as a JS object in `dashboard.tkt` with the dictionary and pattern matcher. Chart.js `backgroundColor` array populated per data point. |

## Epic MK4.10: Advanced Data Type Detection

*The data profiler should detect types beyond numeric/categorical. Detection follows a priority cascade — try the most specific type first, fall back to generic. Users can override any detection via the schema panel dropdown.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK4.10.1 | M | **Done** | **Date/datetime detection + boolean detection + user override** — Detect ISO dates (YYYY-MM-DD), booleans (true/false/yes/no/0/1). Type override dropdown in schema panel persists to sessionStorage. Date columns show range (min→max). |
| MK4.10.2 | M | **Done** | **Advanced type cascade** — Detection order (most specific first): (1) PII by column name (email/phone/name/ssn/dob/address/medicare), (2) Email by regex, (3) Phone by regex, (4) URL by regex, (5) Currency (\.NN pattern), (6) Percentage (NN.N% pattern), (7) ID/key (unique values = row count, often first column), (8) Boolean, (9) Datetime (ISO, AU dd/mm/yyyy, US mm/dd/yyyy, timestamps), (10) Integer (no decimal), (11) Float (has decimal), (12) Categorical (low cardinality text), (13) Free text (high cardinality text, long values). Each type stores relevant stats (e.g., currency: min/max/sum, datetime: range, ID: uniqueness %). |
| MK4.10.3 | S | **Done** | **Type-specific schema display** — Each type gets a distinct icon/colour in the schema panel. Date: calendar icon, range. Currency: dollar icon, sum/mean. Percentage: percent icon. Email/Phone: PII lock. ID: key icon. Boolean: toggle icon with true/false ratio. Free text: text icon with avg length. |
| MK4.10.4 | S | **Done** | **Type detection confidence score** — Each detection reports a confidence (0-1). Show confidence in the schema detail panel. Low-confidence detections (<0.7) get an amber badge suggesting user review. |

*App shell, navigation, settings, and serving infrastructure.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK6.1 | M | **Done** | moke app shell and navigation (sidebar: Datasets / Dashboard / Insight Lab / Pipeline / Settings; dark theme matching loke base.tkt; moke-specific accent colour (teal, distinct from loke indigo); responsive layout; moke version badge in header) |
| MK6.2 | M | **Done** | Multi-provider LLM routing (Claude Sonnet/Haiku via loke's Anthropic provider; GPT-4o-mini via loke's OpenAI provider; model selector in chat panel; local Ollama models when available; API keys from loke keychain (F1.5) — no re-entry in moke settings) |
| MK6.3 | S | **Done** | moke settings page (session memory toggle — clear on close or persist; pipeline console verbosity; default sensitivity level; preferred model; about/version) |
| MK6.4 | M | **Done** | Presentation mode (full-screen dashboard view for demos; hide sidebar; auto-cycle through cards; keyboard arrows to navigate; ESC to exit; timer overlay showing time in presentation; `?` overlay for keyboard shortcuts; used in user research sessions) |
| MK6.5 | S |  | **Nine moke API handlers can never be reached** — Found 2026-09-27 by X7.2, probing the running moke. ooke's `scripts/gen_handlers.sh` registers exactly one `get` and one `post` per page file, which is its documented convention, and moke's handler registry is generated from it. So `pages/api/ml.tk`'s `postrun` and `postpropose`, `pages/api/mcp.tk`'s `gettools`, `postinvoke`, `getbroker`, `getmemory` and `postmemory`, and `pages/api/agents.tk`'s `postscan`, `postschema` and `getdiary` compile but have no route: a request answers 404 or 405. Nothing in the moke UI calls them today, so nothing visible breaks. The Insight Lab's run and propose paths, and the MCP panel's tool invocation, are among them. Either give each its own page file (for example `pages/api/ml/run.tk` exporting `post`), or delete the ones nothing will call. |

---

# Epic T: Test Suite

*Comprehensive behavioural test coverage for every module. Tests verify runtime correctness — not just that files compile, but that functions return expected values for known inputs. Each story produces one or more `.tk` test files under `tests/unit/`, `tests/integration/`, or `tests/e2e/`. Every test file exposes a `run_all():bool` entry point that logs `PASS`/`FAIL` per assertion and returns `true` only if every assertion passes.*

## T1 — Privacy Core

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| T1.1 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-24 |  `tests/unit/privacy/test_regex.tk` — PII detection: email (`john@example.com`), AU phone (`0412 345 678`), US phone (`(555) 123-4567`), credit card (`4111 1111 1111 1111`), AU TFN (`123 456 789`), AU ABN (`51 824 753 556`), API key (`sk-ant-...`), IPv4 (`192.168.1.1`); verify entity_type, sensitivity level, and placeholder format `[TYPE_N]`; verify clean text returns zero entities and `PUBLIC` sensitivity<br><br>**Verdict — untestable (RV1.1, 2026-09-23):** the file this row cites, `tests/unit/privacy/test_regex.tk`, is not in the live tree; it is in `_archived-tests/`, which `scripts/run_tests.sh` excludes from discovery by an explicit `-not -path '*/_archived-tests/*'`. So no run has ever exercised the assertions listed here, in CI or locally, and the Done marker rests on a file the runner cannot see. Reopened for that reason and not because any assertion was disproved: what is missing is the test, and it is named.<br><br>**Done 2026-09-24.** `tests/unit/privacy/test_regex.tk` (new, 55 assertions, green) imports `core.privacy.regex` and asserts entity type, matched value, sensitivity and placeholder by EQUALITY for all eight named types, including the `[TYPE_N]` format and per-type numbering. Non-vacuity checked by MUTATION rather than by inspection: flipping one expected value (`[EMAIL_1]`→`[EMAIL_9]`) produced exactly one failure. **Two of this row's own fixtures are wrong and are asserted in the negative with the measurement.** `123 456 789` is not a TFN — PL1.10 gated `au_tfn` on the real checksum and its weighted sum is 323, not a multiple of 11; `123 456 782` is used for the positive case. `(555) 123-4567` is not a valid US number — `patphoneus` applies the NANP rule that the exchange code starts `[2-9]`, and `123` does not; `(555) 234-5678` is detected. Both are defects in the fixtures, not in the patterns. |

Migration **32** adds `memory_graph_relations.valid_to`, `''` while a relation holds. **The contradiction is defined against the schema, not the row's wording:** the table's primary key is (from, to, kind), so the same relation can never be stored twice with two `valid_to`s. What CAN conflict is the row's "same from + relation": one subject whose relation of a single-valued kind still holds towards two targets at once. The caller names the kind, because only it knows that `employed_by` is single-valued and `knows` is not. An ended relation does not conflict with its successor. **Evidence:** `tests/unit/memory/test_graph.tk` gains 14 assertions. It fails 6 with the kind filter ignoring the kind and contradictions ignoring `valid_to`. |

Mutation (prune comparison disabled) turns 5 red. **Done 2026-09-26.** M1.7g (Done 2026-09-25) decided how an access raises relevance: the sweep ages a memory from the LATER of `created_at` and `accessed_at`, so use extends its life. That replaces a count, which the design does not keep. `tests/unit/memory/test_decay.tk` asserts it: two memories made at the same old time, one used a minute before the sweep; the used one survives and the idle one is evicted. It fails 2 with the sweep ageing by `created_at` alone (measured 2026-09-26). The test comment that still said an access changes nothing is corrected. Every criterion in the row now has an asserted equivalent. |

Reopened for that reason and not because any assertion was disproved: what is missing is the test, and it is named.<br><br>**2026-09-24 (work/tests).** `tests/unit/platform/test_i18n_translator.tk` (new, 19 assertions, green) imports `core.i18n.translator`: `t` reads the compiled-in en-AU table, a missing key returns itself, `tfor` reads en-US explicitly and falls back to en-AU for an unknown code, `tparams` substitutes `{{0}}`/`{{1}}` by index, `setlocale` accepts shipped codes and refuses others, `formatnumber(1234.5)` is `1234.50`. **Real defect found and fixed:** `formatnumber` returned `1234.5` and `0` — `shared.types.f64fixed` truncates but does not pad — so it now pads to two places itself (f64fixed is left alone because the governance dashboard renders and asserts its unpadded form). Seen red against the old code. **No equivalent for** the round trip (`setlocale` cannot make a locale active: there is no durable state to hold it) or singular/plural selection (`tn`'s `_one`/`_other` lookup exists but neither table has a plural entry, so only its fallback is reachable, and that is what is asserted). **Done 2026-09-26.** **Plurals:** both tables gain `loke.entities_zero`, `_one` and `_other`, and `tn` substitutes the count for `{{0}}`. `tn("loke.entities";1)` gives the singular form and `3` gives the plural with its count. **Round trip:** the active locale now has a durable home in `core.storage.settings` under `ui.locale`, as the module's own comment said it should. `setlocalein(store;code)` stores a shipped code and refuses others; it reports success only when the value reads back, because the settings writer's result cannot be discriminated (T-18). `getlocalefrom(store)` reads it, defaulting to en-AU. `tin(store;key)` looks a key up in it. The stateless `t()` and `getlocale()` that the CLI calls are unchanged. **Evidence:** `tests/unit/platform/test_i18n_translator.tk` gains 13 assertions against a real settings store: set en-US, read it back, and `tin` returns the en-US spelling; an unshipped code is refused and leaves the locale in place; switching back works. It fails 5 with `getlocalefrom` ignoring the setting and `tn` not substituting. |

Reopened for that reason and not because any assertion was disproved: what is missing is the test, and it is named.<br><br>**2026-09-24.** `tests/unit/feedback/test_learning.tk` (new, 30 assertions) was written as two deliberate red markers and **both are now fixed, so it is green**. **`deriveadjustments` returned an empty array for every input:** `let result=@();` with no `mut.` and all three branches discarding the value of `result.push(...)` (T-24) — measured zero adjustments for a 0.30-rate routing signal (expected one at −0.2) and a 0.92-rate n=30 signal (expected one at +0.1). The function was inert. **And the only negative-adjustment branch could never fire even once that was fixed:** the guard was `str.contains(featurearea;"routing")` in lower case while a real signal carries the label `arealabel` writes, `"Routing decision"`, and `str.contains` is case-sensitive — so a feedback loop could raise a score and never lower one. Matched on `str.lower(...)` now. **`applylearning` also reported success while persisting nothing:** its INSERT named only `(key, value)` while `settings` declares `value_type` and `updated_at` as NOT NULL with no default, so every write was rejected — and `db.execparams` returns `i64!$lokeerr`, where T-18 makes arm 0 always run, so `ok` was unconditionally true. Now a complete INSERT with `value_type='float'` and `datetime('now')` unquoted in the statement (GA5.1). **Feature-absent, four of five:** no per-model score (every adjustment is the literal `model:"router"`), no PII-pattern confidence (nothing in `core.feedback` reaches `core.privacy`), no sensitivity review flag, no `list_adaptations` and no `revert`.<br><br>**2026-09-24 (work/tests).** Re-checked against HEAD: still absent. `core.feedback.learning` still exports only `computesignals`, `deriveadjustments`, `applylearning`, `formatlearningstate`; every adjustment is `model:"router"`. Stays Partial.<br><br>**2026-09-27.** `core.feedback.learning` gains the missing behaviours that can be built. `modeladjustments` gives each MODEL its own score: at least five ratings, under half thumbs-up, is -0.2; over ten at more than 85% is +0.1. Before this, every adjustment was `model:"router"`. `reviewflags` raises a sensitivity review flag when a governance warning is mostly thumbs-down over ten or more ratings. It flags and does not adjust, because loosening a warning is a policy decision for a person. `recordadaptation` stores each change with its reason, confirmed by read-back; `listadaptations` describes each; `revert` removes one. All go through `std.db` with bools (T-18). **Evidence:** `tests/unit/feedback/test_learning.tk` gains 10 assertions. It fails 4 with per-model scoring collapsed to `router` and `revert` a no-op. **Remaining:** "false-positive PII reduces confidence for that pattern". A feedback row records a feature area, a rating and a comment, but not which entity type or pattern fired, so there is nothing to lower. It needs the privacy-detection feedback to carry the pattern name first. |

Columns whose name marks them personal (`core.optimiser.profiler.ispiiname`) now render `sample=[SUPPRESSED]`. Name-based only; filed as MK21.18. Mutations (old hooks, restore returning its input) turn 6 red. |
