## Epic MK8: Enriched Demo Datasets & Guided Walkthroughs

*Transform demo datasets from minimal samples into rich, realistic data with deliberate patterns for demonstrating every moke capability. Each dataset should have clear outliers, clusters, time series trends, PII examples, and cross-dataset relationships. Add complementary IT operations datasets for a complete observability scenario.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK8.1 | L | **Done** | **IT Server Hardware enrichment** — Expand from 48 to 200 servers across 4 data centres (SYD-1, SYD-2, MEL-1, BNE-1). Add columns: `os_patch_level`, `last_reboot_days`, `cpu_util_avg`, `ram_util_avg`, `disk_util_avg`, `network_throughput_mbps`, `open_incidents`, `maintenance_window`, `environment` (prod/staging/dev/dr), `criticality` (P1-P4), `application_owner`, `cost_centre`. Deliberate patterns: 12 servers at >90% CPU (cluster: overloaded), 8 with expired warranties (cluster: risk), 5 with no recent patches (cluster: vulnerable), 3 in DR never tested. |
| MK8.2 | L | **Done** | **IT Performance Metrics dataset** — New complementary dataset: 10,000 rows of time-series metrics. Columns: `server_id` (links to server hardware), `timestamp`, `cpu_percent`, `ram_percent`, `disk_iops`, `disk_latency_ms`, `network_in_mbps`, `network_out_mbps`, `active_connections`, `error_rate`, `response_time_ms`, `gc_pause_ms`. Deliberate patterns: daily CPU spike 09:00-10:00, gradual memory leak on 3 servers, disk latency degradation before failure, correlated network/error spikes. |
| MK8.3 | L | **Done** | **IT Incidents & Alerts dataset** — New: 500 incident records. Columns: `incident_id`, `server_id`, `severity` (P1-P4), `category` (hardware/software/network/security), `status` (open/investigating/resolved/closed), `opened_at`, `resolved_at`, `mttr_minutes`, `assignee`, `root_cause`, `affected_services`, `change_id`, `related_incidents`, `sla_breached`. Patterns: recurring P1 on 2 servers (same root cause), P2 cluster during maintenance windows, SLA breach rate >15% for network category. |
| MK8.4 | M | **Done** | **IT Change Management dataset** — New: 300 change records. Columns: `change_id`, `type` (standard/normal/emergency), `status` (approved/implemented/rolled_back/failed), `requested_by`, `approved_by`, `implement_date`, `affected_servers`, `risk_level`, `success`, `rollback_reason`, `downtime_minutes`. Patterns: emergency changes correlate with P1 incidents, Friday deployments have 3x rollback rate. |
| MK8.5 | M | **Done** | **IT Application Performance dataset** — New: 2,000 rows. Columns: `app_id`, `app_name`, `endpoint`, `method` (GET/POST/PUT/DELETE), `timestamp`, `response_time_ms`, `status_code`, `error_message`, `user_count`, `request_count`, `apdex_score`, `deployment_version`. Patterns: version 2.3.1 introduced latency regression, one endpoint has 5x error rate, apdex drops during peak hours. |
| MK8.6 | M | **Done** | **IT Asset & Cost dataset** — New: links servers to financial data. Columns: `asset_id`, `server_id`, `purchase_date`, `purchase_cost`, `depreciation_rate`, `current_value`, `monthly_opex`, `power_watts`, `rack_units`, `licence_count`, `licence_cost_monthly`, `support_contract`, `support_expiry`, `vendor`. Patterns: 20% of servers consume 60% of costs, 5 servers past depreciation still running. |
| MK8.7 | S | **Done** | **Cross-dataset relationships** — Define foreign key relationships: server_id links hardware↔metrics↔incidents↔changes↔assets. app_id links performance↔servers (via deployment mapping). Add relationship metadata to dataset registry so moke can suggest joins and cross-dataset queries.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done** — it was not, until MK20.5. The registry and its suggestions were defined in `index.tkt` and called from nowhere, and the `relationships` structure rendered on no page; a function the calling page cannot reach is not a shipped feature. Now in `static/js/moke-relationships.js`, rendered on `/chat`, with every declared join verified against the real columns |
| MK8.8 | L | **Done** | **Guided demo walkthrough** — Add a "Demo" button on the landing page that launches a step-by-step walkthrough: (1) Load IT Server Hardware, (2) View schema with type detection, (3) Ask "Show servers with expired warranties", (4) Navigate to Dashboard → generate dashboard, (5) Navigate to Insight Lab → run clustering, (6) Load IT Performance Metrics, (7) Ask "Which servers have memory leaks?", (8) Navigate to Governance → view session stats. Each step has a prompt chip that auto-fills. Walkthrough tracks progress and can be resumed. |
| MK8.9 | M | **Done** | **Medicare & Water Quality enrichment** — Expand Medicare from 30 to 500 rows with realistic claim patterns (seasonal flu spike in July, bulk billing rate varies by state, gap amounts cluster by specialty). Expand Water Quality from 30 to 200 readings with seasonal temperature variation, 3 sensors showing deteriorating pH trend, 2 turbidity alerts. Add columns: `follow_up_required`, `rebate_percentage` to Medicare. Add `weather_condition`, `rainfall_mm` to Water Quality. |
| MK8.10 | S | **Done** | **Customer Intelligence enrichment** — Expand from 392 to 1,000 customers. Add columns: `acquisition_date`, `last_login_days`, `email_open_rate`, `cart_abandonment_rate`, `preferred_channel` (web/app/store), `lifetime_orders`, `returns_count`, `social_sentiment`. Sharpen clusters: whales (top 2% by LTV), at-risk (high spend + declining frequency), growth (increasing spend trajectory), dormant (>180 days inactive). |

## Epic MK9: API Data Sources & External Connections

*Enable moke to connect to external data sources via REST APIs, GraphQL, and file URLs. This transforms moke from a CSV-upload tool into a data platform that can pull live data from any API endpoint, including Australian open data portals.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK9.1 | L | **Done** | **API connection configuration UI** — New page/panel: "Connect to API". Fields: URL, method (GET/POST/PUT/DELETE), headers (key-value pairs, add/remove rows), query parameters, request body (JSON editor), authentication type (None/API Key/Bearer Token/Basic Auth/OAuth 2.0). Preview button sends test request, shows response status, headers, and first 500 chars of body. Save connection as named data source. |
| MK9.2 | M | **Done** | **REST/JSON data source** — Parse JSON API responses into tabular datasets. Auto-detect: (a) array of objects `[{...},{...}]` → each object is a row, keys are columns, (b) nested `{data: [{...}]}` → unwrap common wrapper patterns, (c) paginated responses → follow `next` links or increment `page` param. Transform options: flatten nested objects (dot notation), select specific JSON paths, rename columns. |
| MK9.3 | M | **Done** | **GraphQL data source** — GraphQL query editor with: endpoint URL, query text area, variables JSON, headers. Execute query, parse response `data` field into tabular format. Introspection query to discover schema. Save queries as named data sources. |
| MK9.4 | S | **Done** | **OpenAPI/Swagger discovery** — Paste an OpenAPI spec URL (JSON or YAML). Parse the spec, list available endpoints with descriptions, parameters, response schemas. Click an endpoint to auto-fill the API connection form. Support Swagger 2.0 and OpenAPI 3.0/3.1. |
| MK9.5 | L | **Done** | **Authentication methods** — Full auth configuration: (a) API Key — header name + value or query param, (b) Bearer Token — static token in Authorization header, (c) Basic Auth — username + password, (d) OAuth 2.0 — client credentials flow: token URL, client ID, client secret, scopes → auto-fetch and refresh access token, (e) Certificate auth — upload client cert + key (PEM), CA cert for mTLS. Show connection health (green/amber/red). Delete connections. Export/import connection configs as JSON (excluding secrets). |
| MK9.9 | S | **Done** | **API response caching** — Cache API responses locally to reduce repeated calls during analysis. Configurable TTL per connection (default 5 minutes). Cache stored in sessionStorage. Show cache hit/miss in pipeline console. Manual cache clear button. |
| MK9.10 | S | **Done** | **Rate limiting and retry** — Respect API rate limits: detect `429 Too Many Requests` and `Retry-After` header. Exponential backoff with configurable max retries (default 3). Show rate limit status in connection manager. Queue concurrent requests to same host. |

# TOOLCHAIN

## Epic F10: Toolchain Currency and Upstream Feedback

*Get loke building on a current toke and ooke, and keep it there, so it cannot drift four months behind again.*

loke's last commit before this work was 2026-05-21. toke was being committed daily and ooke had moved a
major version past loke's declared `ooke >= 1.0.0` without anyone noticing, because nothing checked.

### The hold criterion, and why it is a commit and not a version

**loke needs toke at commit `6cc9061` or later — in practice a build of `main` — and ooke `v3.0.0-rc.1`.**
`TOOLCHAIN.lock` is the authority; this section explains it.

Upstream is explicit that **no tagged toke release works** and that there is no version number to give.
That is not a preference: toke's `VERSION` file still reads 2.8.0 well past the `v2.8.0` tag, so a semver
comparison would happily accept a checkout that cannot build loke. toke is therefore gated on **commit
ancestry** with its version recorded as advisory only. ooke is gated on a **tag**, which it now has.

**Current position, 2026-09-20: the hold is CLEARED.** Both pins are published and fetchable, which was
itself a blocker — neither was on a remote until now, so CI could not obtain a compiler at any migration
status and no figure this project published was reproducible by anyone else. CI now builds loke and
reproduces the local census exactly.

Accepting a release candidate reverses the earlier decision to wait for a final 3.0.0. That reversal was
deliberate and instructed. What does **not** move is the publication bar: a figure measured against an rc
is labelled with the rc, so if the final tag changes, every affected number can be found again.

**The build is green.** 218 of 218 modules compile, 0 errors, and `packages/browser/build/loke` links (`4d6e0ec` onward). The figures this preamble used to carry — "58 of 199 modules, 52 of 179 interfaces, 291 errors of which 226 were E2030 cascade" — were the 2026-09-20 measurement and are kept here only so they are not mistaken for current. Progress was measured in modules compiling and interfaces emitted rather than in error count, which was dominated by cascade and fell in jumps; that was the right instrument and it is why the intermediate F10.14–F10.19 markers quote ladders that do not join up. **The open work is no longer about making the build compile.** It is F10.23 (the 247 files in six trees the build has never touched), F10.17, F10.21, F10.22 and the wiring stories in GA1.
**226 are `E2030` cascade** from modules that have not compiled yet — so the real work is 65 errors in 19
files, split into F10.14–F10.24 below. Three defects in the build itself were masking this and are fixed:
the interface directory held a stale nested tree toke never read, the interface pass omitted
`--emit-llvm` so every library failed on "no main function", and interfaces were emitted alphabetically
rather than in dependency order.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| F10.1 | M | **Done** | **Make the build fail loudly** — every compiler invocation in `build_loke.sh` ended `2>/dev/null \|\| true` and it linked with `-Wl,-undefined,dynamic_lookup`, so a build in which every module failed still printed "Done" and produced a binary, turning undefined symbols into runtime crashes. Now reports a per-module census to `build/break-report.txt` and refuses to link an incomplete build. **Re-verified 2026-09-24 (RV1.5).** Exercised by accident, which is the best kind of evidence. Built against toke main `444a55c`, 21 modules failed to type-check. The build printed the census, wrote `build/trees/loke/break-report.txt`, named the one unresolved symbol and ended "Refusing to link an incomplete build" with exit 1. It did not link. |
| F10.2 | M | **Done** | **Path portability** — `run_tests.sh` and `build_test.sh` hardcoded an absolute home path with no environment override while CI exported `TOKE_DIR`, so **CI's test job could never have passed** regardless of code correctness, despite a badge in the README. Both now honour `TOKE_DIR` and fail fast when the compiler is absent instead of reporting every test as a compile error. |
| F10.3 | M | **Done** | **Pin and gate the toolchain** — `TOOLCHAIN.lock` plus `scripts/check_toolchain.sh`, gating toke by commit ancestry and ooke by released version, per the criterion above. CI clones the pinned commits rather than chasing upstream HEAD, which previously meant CI could break on somebody else's commit at random with no local reproduction. |
| F10.4 | S | **Done** | **Drift watch** — pinning alone would let loke drift silently again, which is how it fell four months behind. A scheduled job compares the lock against upstream HEAD weekly and warns without turning `main` red. |
| F10.5 | M | **Done** | **Diagnostics-driven fixer** — `scripts/fix_diagnostics.py` applies mechanical fixes by byte offset from toke's own JSON diagnostics, so only spans the compiler itself flags are touched. This replaces the regex approach in the previous migration script, whose `^[ \t]*//.*\n` silently deleted the licence header from 520 files. 968 equality fixes applied; `packages/` type-check went 167 to 246 passing. **Re-verified 2026-09-24 (RV1.5).** `scripts/fix_diagnostics.py` exists and patches by byte offset from toke's JSON diagnostics, as stated. The "167 to 246 passing" figure is **historical** (2026-09-19, a different compiler and a different tree) and cannot be re-measured. It must not be quoted as a current figure. It is superseded by the 224-module, 0-error build. |
| F10.6 | S | **Done** | **Remove the destructive migration scripts** — their job is finished and a repo-wide destructive rewriter is a standing hazard whether or not its regex is fixed. |
| F10.7 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-22 | **The 209 ceiling was wrong and is withdrawn.** I reported that eight modules were unreachable because `std.tls` / `std.mdns` / `std.infer` / `std.mlx` declare identifiers containing underscores that toke rejects. They are not unreachable: the compiler carries those functions in its builtin table and resolves them regardless of the `.tki`. Their signatures had drifted, that is all, and all eight now compile. What is true instead is worse for the features and better for the build — **all four of those stdlib modules are stubs** that ignore their arguments and return `0` or a sentinel string (`"[infer: not available]"`, `"[mlx: not available]"`). See T-11 in [`docs/upstream/toke-issues.md`](upstream/toke-issues.md). The affected loke modules now report unavailability instead of treating a stub's return as a result — which mattered: `models/infer.tk` fed `privacy/ner_local.tk`, and a sentinel string reaching a PII detector reads as "no entities found".<br><br>Getting from compiling to **linking** was a second phase and larger than the first. Every module compiled while clang rejected the linked IR, with errors that name an LLVM label and no toke source file. Seven toke defects were isolated with minimal reproductions and are filed as T-1 to T-7; four sweeps in `scripts/` now find each class in source or IR, and all five are gate steps. The largest was **T-6: any `let` that shadows an enclosing `let` emits invalid IR** — which is also how a broken accumulator looks, so the same 52 sites were both a link failure and a silent logic bug.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done** — **217 of 217 modules compile and `packages/browser/build/loke` links.** First green build of this repository. 308 errors → 0 *(Ladder note, settled 2026-09-23: 308 → 0 is the END STATE after every one of F10.14–F10.24 landed, not a rung in the same ladder as their individual steps. It is a summary figure and should not be read as a single story's delta.)* |

The compiler resolves those functions from its builtin table irrespective of the `.tki`, so they are reachable; all 217 modules compile. | F10.30 | S | **Done** — cause corrected, 2026-09-24 | **BSD `grep -r` from the repo root silently skips `src/dist/`** — Found 2026-09-23 (F10.17). It walks 700 `.tk` where `find` sees 706: `grep -rl 'std.string' .` reports nothing in `src/dist`, while `grep -l 'std.string' src/dist/*.tk` returns four files. That is why a naive count gave 67 imports instead of 71, and it plausibly explains why **six files in `src/dist/` have no `Copyright 2026 loke contributors` header** — pre-existing gate violations that the repair sweep after the 520-file header-stripping migration never saw. Two jobs: restore the six headers, and audit every sweep script in `scripts/` that starts from `.` to use a `find`-driven file list instead. Any measurement this project has taken with a root `grep -r` is suspect until that is done<br><br>**Closed 2026-09-24, and the cause in this row is wrong — which matters more than the symptom.** The skip is real and reproducible: `grep -rl "m=" .` from the repo root finds **0** files under `./src/dist` while `grep -rl "m=" src/dist` finds **6**. But it is not BSD grep. `grep` on this machine is **ugrep 7.8.4**, which honours `.gitignore` for a recursive search rooted at a directory and lets an explicit path override — and `.gitignore:5` is `dist/`, confirmed by `git check-ignore -v src/dist`. That also explains this row's own "700 `.tk` where `find` sees 706" **exactly**: the difference is 6, which is precisely the number of `.tk` files under `src/dist`. **The consequence is wider than one directory:** every recursive `grep` in this repository silently skips every gitignored path, so any measurement taken that way is a lower bound. In practice what is ignored is build output plus these six legacy files, so nothing published is wrong — but the method is. **Nothing in the toolchain is affected**, checked: no script uses a bare recursive `grep` from the root, and every sweep in `scripts/` walks the tree with Python's `pathlib.rglob`, which does not read `.gitignore`. For a human at a prompt: pass an explicit path, or `--no-ignore`. |
| F10.31 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-26 | **The interface pass has no resilience to a mid-graph failure** — Found 2026-09-23 (F10.23). It is a single pass in dependency order, so one broken module blinds everything above it: a transient break in `core.privacy.regex` took **126 of 210** interfaces with it, which then presented as 126 modules' worth of `E2030 module not found`. Two consequences worth naming. It makes a census wildly misleading in exactly the situation where you most need it — the `N of M interface(s) emitted` line is the leading indicator and the census advice now says to read it first. And it nearly put a fabricated number into a baseline. The real fix is to iterate to a fixpoint, or to emit signatures without type-checking bodies; the latter is an upstream ask **Done 2026-09-26: iterate to a fixpoint.** After the ordered pass, `build_tree.sh` retries only the modules whose `.tki` is still missing, up to 8 passes, and stops when a pass adds none. A genuinely broken module stays missing, and the census reports it as before. **Evidence, measured:** with the first pass deliberately run in REVERSE dependency order, standing in for an early module failing, the old single pass emitted **48 of 204** interfaces and the build failed. With the fixpoint, pass 2 reached **204 of 204** and the build succeeded. In normal order the extra pass costs nothing, because nothing is missing. Emitting signatures without type-checking bodies remains the upstream ask. |

The upstream ask is an instance of T-7 (an unknown member call is unchecked), so it is not filed separately. Other bare `i=std:std;` imports call only `std.homedir()`, which links. |
| F10.35 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-26 | **A reader of `build/interfaces` can see a half-written directory, so no concurrent measurement is trustworthy** — Diagnosed 2026-09-23 after three separate workers reported intermittent `E2030 module 'shared.log' not found; available:` with an **empty** available-list on files they had not touched, and after one full-suite run reported 20 phantom failures. `build_tree.sh` takes a `mkdir`-atomic per-target lock, which correctly serialises **writer against writer** — but step 1 does `rm -rf "$IFACE_DIR"` and the pass then repopulates it incrementally, reading its own output as it goes (`-I "$IFACE_DIR"` while emitting into it). A **reader** — every `toke -I build/interfaces` type-check, which is how every worker verifies a single file — takes no lock and sees whatever is there. Measured mid-build: **48 interfaces of ~200**. So the failure is not in the code being checked, and a green suite taken while any build is in flight means nothing. It also nearly put a fabricated count into a baseline. Because the pass must read what it has already emitted, a plain temp-dir-and-rename will not do: emit into a staging directory, then make `build/interfaces` a **symlink** swapped with `ln -sfn`, which is a single atomic rename — a reader then follows the link to either the old complete set or the new complete set, never a partial one. Two consumers reference the path directly (`run_tests.sh`, CI) and follow a symlink transparently. Related to F10.31, which is the same directory failing for a different reason **Done 2026-09-26, as the row designed it.** `build_tree.sh` emits into `<iface>.stage.<pid>`. Once the pass is complete, it publishes with `ln -sfn` plus a rename that replaces the link itself (`mv -h` on BSD, `-T` on GNU): one rename(2). The previous staging directory is then removed. A real directory left by an older build is replaced once. **Evidence, measured:** sampling `ls build/interfaces/*.tki` every 0.2 s during a full build saw **204 on all 248 samples** with the new script. With the old script it saw **min 0, max 204** over 265 samples, so a reader could find an empty directory mid-build. |
| F10.36 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **ooke objects compiled by an older toke crashed the server on every API request** — Found 2026-09-24 by RV1.2. Starting `packages/browser/build/loke` and requesting `/api/health`, `/api/models` or `/api/pipeline` gave an empty reply, and the pages `/privacy`, `/savings` and `/pipeline` did the same. Each left a macOS crash report showing a jump to address 0 inside the forked `worker_loop`. The faulting instruction was `blr x9` in `tk_str_toint_w`: a call through the thread-local thunk for `tk_current_error`, and the thunk was 0. The cause: `build_tree.sh` linked ooke by re-assembling the IR files already in `$OOKE_DIR/src/ooke/`, emitted on 2026-09-20 by an older toke. All twelve declared `@tk_current_error = external global i64`, but the runtime at the pin defines it `thread_local` (toke 127.101). ld accepted the mismatch silently. ooke's code reached the symbol through the GOT (`GOTLDP` relocations) and wrote 0 over the TLV descriptor, so the next thread-local access jumped to 0. **Every unit test passed throughout**, because tests call handlers directly and never enter the accept loop. **Fixed:** `build_tree.sh` now compiles ooke from source with the same `$TOKE` into `build/ooke-objs`, rebuilding when the sources or the compiler change. It refuses to link if any ooke IR still declares the symbol without `thread_local`, and it no longer writes into the shared ooke checkout, which it used to modify under other builds. `run_tests.sh` takes ooke's archive from the same place. The objects now use `TLVLDP` relocations. **Guarded** by `scripts/smoke_serve.sh`, in the gate's test step, which makes one real request per route. It was proven red with the old objects relinked: the server never answered, and it also ignored the configured port, a second symptom of the same mismatch. After the fix, all 13 routes answered and no crash report was written |
| F10.37 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **A child process was waited for before its output was read, so `/sysmon` deadlocked, and it read no process on any Mac** — Found 2026-09-24 when `/sysmon` hung the loke server intermittently. `core.monitoring.sysmon.getprocessstats` called `process.wait` and then `process.stdout`. Measured, the `ps` listing was 64,608 bytes against a 65,536-byte pipe buffer, so once the process list grew a little, ps blocked on a full pipe while loke waited for it to exit. The same order held in nine places: five in sysmon, three in moke's sysmon page, and one each in the installer's version check and the CLI updater's and signer's `runcmd`. It also asked `ps` for `nlwp`, a Linux-only column; macOS drops it, which left four fields where the parser required more than four. And an idle process could never replace the empty starting value, so a sleeping Ollama read as pid 0. **Fixed:** every site reads stdout and stderr to EOF before waiting, `ps -A -c -o pid=,pcpu=,rss=,comm=` (24,901 bytes), `>=4`, and an empty best is always replaced. **Guarded** by the new `scripts/find_wait_before_read.py` in the gate: it flags the old sysmon at 6 sites and finds 0 now. **Evidence:** `tests/unit/monitoring/test_sysmon_ps.tk` finds launchd as pid 1 and a missing process as 0. Against the old module the test binary did not finish, and was killed by a 20-second alarm with seven `ps -eo …nlwp` children still blocked. That is the deadlock, observed. |

# DEMO LAYER — moke: DATASETS AND PERSONAL USE

> **Scope note.** Everything in this section is **moke**, the demo application, not loke core. Demo
> datasets, guided flows, import paths and the personal-finance workspace all live in
> `packages/moke/`. loke core gains nothing dataset-specific from these epics — where a story needs a
> core capability it depends on an NC1, DA1 or AD1 story rather than adding domain logic to core.
> The test corpora under `tests/fixtures/` are the exception and are deliberately core: they test the
> detector, not the demo.

## Epic MK19: Government Open Data Expansion

*Broaden the demo beyond synthetic data with real, attributed, offline-first snapshots of Australian government open data, each arriving complete with its own prompts, dashboard and flow.*

The current 15 datasets are synthetic despite being labelled "Government Open Data", and the enrichment
recorded in MK8 landed only in the frontend, so the backend still holds pre-enrichment row counts. Three
different wrong dataset counts appear across the docs. This epic replaces guesswork with attributed
snapshots and one manifest that drives everything.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK19.1 | M | **Partial** | **Decide the canonical store and stop the split** — Two divergent dataset stores exist: `packages/moke/data/*.tk` with `data/registry.tk`, served by `pages/api/datasets.tk`, and a `var DATASETS` object inline in `templates/index.tkt` which is **230KB of data and UI fused together**. `grep "api/moke/datasets"` returns zero hits, so the backend path is orphaned and the frontend object is what the demo uses. The two hold different dataset sets. Pick one — recommended: a static manifest plus CSV snapshots under `static/data/`, fetched lazily, because it needs no recompilation, is diffable and reviewable, and drops the weight of `/` from 230KB to about 25KB. Also fix `registry.tk::listall()`, which has an `if` where a loop belongs and therefore returns only one AU dataset.<br><br>**Status detail:** Partial — `static/js/moke-data.js` is the canonical data layer (manifest, lazy CSV fetch, value-free schema profile, and the `MokeActive` shim that lets both stores coexist safely). 23 tests pass against the real fixtures. **The inline 230KB object in `index.tkt` is not yet replaced** and the 17 direct sessionStorage call sites are not yet migrated |
| MK19.2 | M | **Done** — manifest schema implemented and populated by the fetch script; `packages/moke/static/data/manifest.json` | **Manifest-driven dataset metadata** — One entry per dataset carrying id, name, description, domain, **kind** (`snapshot` / `synthetic` / `generated`), provenance, sensitivity, PII columns, per-column descriptions, relationships, **suggested prompts**, and a **pre-baked dashboard**. This replaces six hardcoded touch points: the hand-written cards in `index.tkt`, the `DATASETS` object, `DASH_PROMPTS` in `dashboard.tkt`, the prompt map in `chat.tkt`, `suggestCrossQueries`, and `DEMO_WALKTHROUGH` in `base.tkt`. A new dataset should arrive complete rather than needing edits in four files. |
| MK19.3 | M | **Done** | **Provenance and licence attribution** — Each snapshot ships publisher, source URL, retrieval date, verbatim licence name and URL, an attribution string, a SHA-256, and a **`transformations` list naming every edit made to the raw file**. Committing a government CSV to a public repository is redistribution, so attribution is a licence obligation, not tidiness. Extend the repo `NOTICE` and surface attribution in the UI.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done** — provenance, verbatim licence, SHA-256 and a `transformations` list per snapshot, plus a licence sidecar and an extended `NOTICE` |
| MK19.4 | M | **Done** — `scripts/fetch_opendata.py` with the licence guard, verified firing on a deliberate mismatch | **Reproducible fetch script** — No dataset generator exists. Add one driven by a recipe file: resolve the resource through the portal API, apply column selection and row caps, enforce a size budget, write the CSV plus a licence sidecar, compute the hash, and patch the manifest. **Abort if the portal's declared licence does not match the recipe's expected licence** — that guard is what stops an unattributable or non-redistributable file reaching git. Add a `--check` mode that reports drift without writing. |
| MK19.5 | L | **Partial** | **First four datasets, one per domain** — Health (emergency department or elective surgery waiting times, aggregate only), energy and environment (electricity price and demand, or daily climate observations), transport (road deaths), economy (labour force). Chosen for a clear analytical story and an unambiguous licence. **Verify each licence individually from the portal's own metadata rather than assuming CC-BY**: some energy market data is published under terms that do not permit redistribution, in which case it ships as live-fetch-only with a manifest entry and no committed snapshot.<br><br>**Status detail:** Partial — transport done (BITRE road fatalities, cc-by, 4,000 rows). **ABS publishes no CSV**, so the economy dataset is blocked on the XLSX gap in `toke-libraries-required.md`; AEMO is flagged live-fetch-only pending a terms review; health still to do |
| MK19.6 | M | **Done** | **Distinguish real from synthetic in the UI** — The existing `au.*` datasets are labelled "Government Open Data" and contain fabricated names and identifiers. Badge them `synthetic`, and badge the new snapshots `real`. The most persuasive privacy demonstration in the whole product is the contrast: a real public aggregate where the filter finds nothing, against a synthetic patient fixture where it finds everything, on the same pipeline, back to back.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done** — but only half was, until MK20.3 needed it. The `real` badge was applied; **`.badge-synthetic` was defined and used nowhere**, so six cards sat under a bare "Government Open Data" heading with no marking — including "Medicare Benefits Schedule", described as "MBS claims data with patient, provider, and billing information", which reads as real health data. That is precisely the misrepresentation this story exists to prevent. The badge is now on all six, and the heading says "Synthetic Fixtures" with a sentence stating every name and identifier is fabricated. The section hides itself if the manifest is unavailable, so a failure degrades the page rather than breaking it. |
| MK19.7 | M | **Done** | **Pre-baked dashboards so demos never need a live model** — Ship a dashboard definition per dataset, rendered through the existing local resolution path with no provider call. Constrain every card to the operations the resolver actually implements, and validate column names against the snapshot header in CI — a typo produces a blank card on stage with no error.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done** — pre-baked dashboard for the road-fatalities snapshot, 12 cards, rendered locally with no provider call. `--validate-dashboards` checks every column and operation against the real snapshot header and against the resolver's actual op set, and refuses baked-in values. Verified it catches a typo'd column, an unsupported operation and a baked value |
| MK19.8 | M |  | **Wire the orphaned connection modules** — Nine modules totalling ~199KB in `static/js/` are referenced only by a standalone test runner; `templates/connections.tkt` has one inline script and empty placeholder comments where they should mount, and its "Load as Dataset" button is disabled with an error string saying a module is unimplemented that is in fact written. Mount them and make live refresh a separately demonstrated feature, never the demo path. **Note before mounting:** `datagov-source.js` had the wrong CKAN base URL (`/api/3/action`, which returns 404 with an HTML body; the correct base is `/data/api/3/action`). That is now fixed, but it is a warning about the rest — these modules have never been exercised against a live endpoint, so mounting them is the beginning of the work rather than the end of it. |
| MK19.9 | L |  | **The `/api/proxy` endpoint they all depend on** — `/api/proxy` does not exist, and six call sites reference it, so the existing "Test Connection" button has never worked either. It is an **SSRF vector by construction**: a local service fetching an arbitrary caller-supplied URL, on a machine also running loke, Ollama and a database. Non-negotiable: a default-deny host allowlist, rejection of localhost, link-local, cloud metadata and all private ranges, HTTPS only, a body-size cap and timeout, and no cross-host redirect without re-checking the allowlist. Needs its own security review and a toke change, so it is gated on the toolchain. |
| MK19.10 | S | **Done** | **Correct the dataset counts** — README, `features-moke.md` and `registry.tk` state three different numbers, none of which is 15. Fix all of them and add the count to the manifest so it is derived rather than asserted.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done** — verified the real count (15 synthetic keys in the `DATASETS` object, plus 1 real snapshot) and corrected `README.md` to state the synthetic-versus-real split explicitly rather than hiding it behind "Australian-themed". Row counts now live in the manifest so a figure cannot drift from the data. |

## Epic MK21: Personal Finance Workspace

*The demo where the no-custody claim is tested to destruction: import your own bank statements, structure and analyse them entirely on-device, and use the model to build the analysis without ever showing it the data.*

**Why this epic matters more than the others.** Every other dataset in moke is public or synthetic, so
"no data left the device" is a claim nobody has to trust. A bank statement is the opposite: it is
genuinely the user's, it is maximally sensitive, and nobody would accept "we redact it first" as an
answer — which is precisely the argument the external literature supports
(`docs/research/disclosure-measurement-findings.md` §1). If any personal financial value leaves the
device during a session in this workspace, the architecture claim in NC1 has failed. This workspace is
therefore both the flagship demonstration and the hardest test.

**What the model is for, and what it is never given.** The model helps *build the analysis* — proposing
a category taxonomy, generating the query or rule set, explaining what a tax category generally covers.
It is never given a transaction, a merchant, an amount, an account number or a balance. Merchant names
in particular are not "low sensitivity": a pharmacy, a clinic, a solicitor or a gambling site each
discloses something material about a person, so merchant text is treated as personal data throughout and
never leaves, not even for categorisation.

> **Two things this epic must say plainly to the user, not bury.**
>
> 1. **Storage is not encrypted today.** The database encryption pragma is inert (X8), so imported
>    statements would sit in plaintext on disk. Personal financial data makes that materially worse than
>    it is for demo data. Either X8 lands first, or this workspace keeps its data in a separately
>    encrypted store, or it refuses to persist at all and works per-session. **MK21.2 decides this, and
>    it gates the rest of the epic.**
> 2. **This is not tax advice, and it does not lodge anything.** It organises figures and shows its
>    working so a person or their accountant can check it. Any output that looks like a determination
>    must be labelled as an estimate with its method shown.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| MK21.1 | M |  | **Workspace scaffolding and the disclosure contract** — A distinct workspace with its own explicit contract shown on entry: what is read, what is computed locally, what is sent (a schema profile and a question), and what is never sent. State the storage position from MK21.2 honestly rather than in a tooltip. Everything in this workspace defaults to no-custody; the redaction fallback is not offered here at all, because for this data it is not an acceptable second choice. |
| MK21.2 | L |  | **Decide and implement at-rest protection — gates the epic** — Choose between waiting for X8, using a separately encrypted store via `std.encrypt` (verified present: `aes256gcmencrypt`/`decrypt`/`keygen`/`noncegen` and `hkdfsha`) with a key derived through the existing keychain path, or running session-only with nothing written to disk. Recommended while X8 is outstanding: **session-only by default**, with persistence an explicit opt-in that states what it writes and where. Also provide a single, verifiable "delete everything" that actually removes files rather than dropping rows, and prove it by inspecting the filesystem afterwards. *Note 2026-09-23: `std.encrypt` is at the pin and X8 is unblocked, so the at-rest decision that gates this epic no longer waits on a capability — only on a choice. See X8.2.* |
| MK21.3 | L |  | **CSV import with column mapping** — Bank CSV exports differ by institution in column order, header naming, date format, and whether amounts are signed in one column or split across debit and credit. Build an import step that proposes a mapping (date, description, amount or debit/credit, balance, currency) and lets the user correct it before anything is accepted. toke provides `std.csv` with a reader and writer, so this path has a binding. On the frontend, reuse one of the three existing `parseCSV` implementations rather than adding a fourth. *Note 2026-09-23: `std.csv` was hardened in the same delivery — 18 exports now, adding `dialect`, `raggedreport` and `lasterrline`. A ragged row and the line it occurred on are both reportable, which is what a bank CSV importer needs to tell a user WHICH row it could not read rather than failing the file.* |
| MK21.3a | M |  | **Unblocked 2026-09-23 — the capability is in the PINNED commit.** **XLSX import** — This row said *"there is no XLSX or zip binding in toke (`std.zip` and `std.xlsx` do not [exist])"*. Both now exist. **Corrected 2026-09-23 (later the same day): BOTH are at the current pin and no lock bump is needed.** This row was written when the pin was `445f519`, and said `std.zip` was present there while `std.xlsx` had landed later at `43899e7`, off `main`, so using it would need a deliberate `TOOLCHAIN.lock` bump. The lock has since moved to `0b0bc4e` (toke's Epic 135 delivery, verified on `origin/main`), and `git cat-file -e 0b0bc4e:stdlib/<m>.tki` confirms **all six** of `std.zip`, `std.xlsx`, `std.pdf`, `std.ocr`, `std.image` and `std.csv` are present at the pin itself — checked against the commit, not against the working checkout, which is ahead of it. Function counts match toke's delivery note exactly: CSV 18, PDF 11, Zip 6, XLSX 8, OCR 12, Image 15. `docs/toke-libraries-required.md:45-46` still lists both as asks and should be closed. Note MK5.1 is marked Done claiming "SheetJS-equivalent via `std.xlsx`" — that was false when written and is only now possible. **MK21.4/MK21.4a stay genuinely blocked**: there is no `std.pdf` and no `std.ocr`<br><br>**Status detail:** **Unblocked 2026-09-23 — the capability arrived** *toke Epic 135 delivered `f7d6c9c..0b0bc4e` on origin/main and `TOOLCHAIN.lock` now pins `0b0bc4e`, so `std.zip` (6 exports) and `std.xlsx` (8, including `datefromserial` and `datesystem`) are available at the pin rather than after it. No lock bump is outstanding and nothing here waits on a capability.* |
| MK21.4 | L |  | **Unblocked 2026-09-23 — the capability is in the PINNED commit.** **Two extraction paths, and do not conflate them** — Many statements are **text PDFs** where characters can be extracted directly with no recognition at all, and only scanned or photographed statements need OCR. Detect which and always prefer extraction, because it is exact and OCR is not. Specify the detection rule and the fallback, and record which path produced each field so MK21.5 can weight confidence accordingly. **Unblocked 2026-09-23 — the capability arrived.** *`std.pdf` now exists: 11 functions including `pdf.openfile`, `pdf.pagecount`, `pdf.pagetext`, `pdf.hastext` and `pdf.runs`. `hastext` is the one that decides this story — it distinguishes a text-layer PDF from a scan, which is exactly the fork between the two extraction paths. Landed **after** the pinned `445f519`, so using it needs a deliberate `TOOLCHAIN.lock` bump.* *`std.pdf` at the pin, 11 exports. `pdf.hastext` is the one that decides this story — it distinguishes a text-layer PDF from a scan directly, which is the fork between the two extraction paths, and we had proposed detecting it ourselves by extracting and measuring what came back. A direct predicate removes a heuristic.*<br><br>**Re-verified 2026-09-24 against the pin, with signatures.** Checked with `git show 0b0bc4e:stdlib/pdf.tki`, so these are the shapes at the pinned commit rather than at the working checkout. The fork this story is about is one call: **`pdf.hastext(pdfdoc, i64) -> bool`**, per PAGE — which matters, because a scanned page bound into an otherwise text PDF is the common real case and a per-document flag would mis-handle it. The text path is `pdf.pagetext(pdfdoc, i64) -> str!pdferr`; `pdf.runs(pdfdoc, i64) -> @(textrun)!pdferr` gives positioned runs, which is what field-level extraction with coordinates needs and is therefore the one MK21.5's confidence weighting should read. Page geometry is `pdf.pagewidth`/`pageheight` (f64), `pdf.pagecount(pdfdoc) -> i64`, and `pdf.close(pdfdoc) -> void` — so the document is a resource with a lifetime and needs closing on every path, including the error ones. **Two implementation notes that are the likeliest way to get this wrong.** Every one of these returns a stdlib result (`!pdferr`), so **`$ok` must be written first** — T-31: arms bind by position, and an error arm written first inverts the match, so a page that extracted fine would run the failure path. And an extraction that yields an EMPTY string must not be treated as "no text, therefore no PII": that is a fail-open on the privacy path, and it is exactly what `hastext` is for — if `hastext` is true and `pagetext` returns empty, that is a defect to surface, not a clean document. The lock note in this row is stale: no bump is needed, the capability is at `0b0bc4e`. |
| MK21.4a | XL |  | **Unblocked 2026-09-23 — the capability is in the PINNED commit.** **On-device extraction sidecar — the binding does not exist** — toke has **no OCR and no PDF capability**. It does, however, already have the *preprocessing* primitives: `std.image` decodes and encodes PNG, JPEG, WebP and BMP and provides `tograyscale`, `crop`, `resize`, `pixelat` and `fromraw`, all from a single dependency-free `image.c`. What is missing is recognition itself, and PDF entirely. So the extraction step cannot be built in toke today, though less of it is missing than it first appears. The right answer is a **local sidecar process**, following the precedent already in the repository at `packages/privacy-filter/` — a small local service loke talks to over loopback. That keeps extraction on-device, which is what matters here, without waiting on an upstream binding. Candidate engines, both local: the platform text recogniser on macOS, or a bundled Tesseract for cross-platform. **The acceptance criterion is the privacy one, not the accuracy one: prove no network call occurs during extraction**, by running a full import with networking disabled and asserting it still succeeds. A cloud OCR service would defeat the entire point of the workspace, so engine choice here is a privacy decision. **Known risk from the precedent:** the existing sidecar's checked-in virtualenv lacks its own dependencies and cannot run, so this story owns a working install path and a health check, not just a server file. **Unblocked 2026-09-23 — the capability arrived.** *And it needs no sidecar at all. `std.ocr` binds **macOS Vision from plain C99** — 12 functions, 910 lines, including `ocr.isavailable`, `ocr.textfile`, `ocr.runs` (with per-run geometry, which MK21.5's confidence surfacing needs), `ocr.languages` and `ocr.setfast`. That is decision **O** from the 2026-09-20 plan — the macOS text recogniser — built upstream rather than here, so the separate OCR repository sketched in `docs/toke-libraries-required.md` is unnecessary. Landed after the pin.* *And it needs no sidecar. `std.ocr` at the pin, 12 exports, binding macOS Vision from plain C99 (ADR-0015) — which is decision **O** from the 2026-09-20 plan, built upstream. `std.image` also arrived, 15 exports including **`adaptivethreshold`** — the preprocessing step `docs/toke-libraries-required.md` §5 had scoped as five stories of our own work including a trained glyph model. That whole section is now unnecessary and the standalone OCR repository it proposed should not be built.*<br><br>**Re-scoped 2026-09-24 — this row's premise is now false, and most of it is cancellable.** It says *"toke has **no OCR and no PDF capability**"* and concludes that *"the right answer is a **local sidecar process**"*. Both `std.ocr` (12 functions) and `std.pdf` (11) are present at the pinned `0b0bc4e`, verified against the commit. **Recognition is in-process, so the sidecar is not needed** — and that removes the whole of this story's cost: a second process to ship, supervise, health-check, version and keep off the network. What survives is a thin module, which is MK21.4's size, not XL. The API makes the availability question a real predicate rather than a probe: `ocr.isavailable() -> bool` and `ocr.engine() -> str`, so loke can *say which engine produced a figure* instead of inferring it — which is what this epic needs for provenance. Also `ocr.setlanguages(str) -> bool`, `ocr.setfast(bool) -> void`, `ocr.runs(@(byte)) -> @(ocrrun)!ocrerr` (positioned, like `pdf.runs`), `ocr.text`/`ocr.textfile` for the whole-page form. The preprocessing survey in this row is also out of date in loke's favour: `std.image` now has **`image.adaptivethreshold(imgbuf, u32, f64) -> imgbuf!str`**, plus `blur`, `convolve`, `rotate` and `tiffdecode`/`tiffpages` — binarisation and deskew are the two steps that decide whether OCR on a photographed page is usable, and both are now available without a dependency. **What does NOT change: `ocr.isavailable()` returning false must fail the extraction, not return an empty result.** A scanned document that silently extracts to nothing would pass the privacy filter as clean, which is the fail-open NC1.9 exists to prevent, one layer up. Decide explicitly whether an un-OCR-able document is refused or quarantined, and assert it. |

For k-means that means rewriting the arithmetic, so it is filed rather than forced. `core.optimiser.cache.tof64` returns `@f64` and is SAFE, because its accumulator is only ever self-updated and so takes the in-place path — that is the discriminator for anyone fixing it. **`moke_costcomparison` was the fifth and is FIXED**: `'%t93' defined with type 'double' but expected 'i32'`, where `let incost=(tokensin / 1000) * costin` divided an `i32` by 1000 as an INTEGER — so every request under 1,000 tokens was costed at zero and every other rounded down to a whole thousand. A multi-line address is ordinary data in exactly the kind of spreadsheet this demo loads. **Scope:** replace `parsecsv`'s internals with a `csv.reader` loop, keep `guessname`/`validate`/`gencolheaders`, and keep `padfields` only if `csv.raggedreport` does not cover it. Note `csv.reader` takes `[byte]` and a `u8` delimiter, so the plumbing differs from `str`-based splitting. Assert the multi-line-quoted-field case first, red, before touching the parser — that is the only assertion that distinguishes the new code from the old. Every csv.* call returns a stdlib result, so **`$ok` first** (T-31).<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-24, and the defect was worse than the row predicted.** The row said a multi-line quoted field would shift every subsequent column. # AUDIT-DRIVEN GAPS

> **Generated:** 2026-05-20 — Full audit of specification, design principles, and architecture against actual implementation. These epics address gaps where the vision has not aligned with what was built.

## Epic GA1: Pipeline Integration — Wire Core Engine into Browser

*Written when the browser API handler bypassed the core engine entirely. **Five of the nine wirings now exist at HEAD** — privacy pipeline, response restoration, kill switch, audit logging and policy evaluation. Four do not: the optimiser, the semantic cache, the router (the provider is still chosen by an if/else on API-key length), and therefore the end-to-end verification. Corrected 2026-09-19 against the committed tree; see `docs/claims.md` A13 for the line-level evidence.*

> **Do not read the working tree for this epic's status.** The uncommitted copy of `pipeline.tk` strips all nine core imports and is a regression, not the state of the repository (F10.13, X8.5).

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| GA1.1 | L | **Done** | **Wire privacy pipeline into browser handler** — Replace the stub string-matching in `pipeline.tk` with actual calls to `core.privacy.pipeline.run()`. Import the privacy pipeline module, run all text through regex + NER + Presidio layers, generate reversible placeholders, and send only anonymised text to LLM providers. This is the **#1 critical fix** — currently ALL browser traffic flows unfiltered to external LLMs. **Exercised end to end 2026-09-25** by `scripts/smoke_e2e.sh` (RV1.4). |
| GA1.2 | M | **Done** | **Wire response restoration into browser handler** — After receiving LLM response, call `core.privacy.placeholder.restore()` to swap placeholders back to original values before returning to the user. Currently the browser returns raw LLM output with no restoration. The CLI proxy (`cli/src/proxy.tk`) does this correctly — use it as reference.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done — 2026-09-23.** Recovered under GA1.10. The reply's text is extracted with the typed `json.arr` accessor, restored into **plain text** via `ph.restore` over `ph.mapfromentities(res.entities)`, and escaped afterwards — so a restored value containing a quote cannot malform the response, which restoring over the raw JSON body would have risked. The response now carries `response` (restored, what the user reads) and `anthropic_raw` (the anonymised body the provider returned) as distinct fields, on purpose **Exercised end to end 2026-09-25** by `scripts/smoke_e2e.sh` (RV1.4). |
| GA1.3 | M | **Done** | **Wire kill switch check before LLM dispatch** — Before any outbound LLM request in `pipeline.tk`, call `core.governance.kill_switch.isengaged()`. If engaged, return an error response explaining why and when it will auto-release. Currently the kill switch is implemented but never checked — a user who trips it can continue sending data unprotected. |
| GA1.4 | M | **Done** | **Wire audit logging into request path** — After each LLM call, call `core.storage.audit.logevent()` with: event type, model, provider, sensitivity, token counts (in/out), cost estimate, duration, and correlation ID. Currently `audit.tk` is never called from any request path. Note its hashing is **not** tamper-evident — the stored digest is a concatenation of two non-secret fields and does not incorporate the previous record; see GA5.2.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done — 2026-09-23.** Recovered under GA1.10, wired at the route rather than the dispatcher: `core.providers.dispatcher` is imported by three modules, **none route-reachable**, so auditing there would log zero events for real traffic. Uses the new `audit.insertevent` (plain bool), not `logevent`, because logevent re-wraps a correctly discriminated stdlib result in a user sum and T-18 then makes a failed insert read as a success — a chain attesting to a history with a hole. A failed provider call is audited too. Asserted by three new integration assertions including a **row-count delta of exactly one**. Cost is recorded as 0.0 rather than estimated, because `calccost` returns an f64 across a module boundary (LS1.1h) **Exercised end to end 2026-09-25** by `scripts/smoke_e2e.sh` (RV1.4). |
| GA1.4a | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **The hosted request body named no model and carried no prompt** — found while sweeping T-31, and it is why nothing downstream of a cloud request had ever been observed working. Both providers built their body as `json.enc($jsonobj{model:…; maxtokens:…; messages:…})`, and **`$jsonobj` is declared in NO module in this repository**. `json.enc` takes a `str`, so the expression compiles, links, and returns a string containing neither the model id, nor `"messages"`, nor the user's text — measured on the identical expression in `core.router.intent`. On the live path: `dispatcher:57-66` to `pipeline.dispatch`. Replaced by `pt.anthropicbody` / `pt.openaibody`, built with `str.concat` and using `json.enc` for the one thing it is right for, escaping a str — a prompt is the field most likely to contain a quote or a newline. Two defects were hiding behind it and are fixed with it: the wire field is `max_tokens`, not `maxtokens`, on both APIs, and Anthropic takes `system` as a top-level string rather than as a message with role "system". |
| GA1.5 | M | **Reopened** — no optimiser call exists in the handler | **Wire token optimisation into pipeline** — Before sending to LLM, run prompt through `core.optimiser.toon.encode()` for structured data and/or `core.optimiser.llmlingua.compress()` for natural language. Report compression ratio in pipeline console. Target: 60-80% token reduction per spec. Currently TOON and LLMLingua are fully implemented but dormant.<br><br>**Status detail — 2026-09-23, and a correction.** Commit `77219c5`'s message names GA1.5 for work that is not this story. What landed in `forward()` is the BUDGET check (`budget.check` → `bcheck.allowed`), which no story owns and which T14.2 exercises. **GA1.5 as written — `toon.encode` / `llmlingua.compress` in the request path — is still not done**: grepping `pipeline.tk` for toon, llmlingua or compress returns nothing. It is also not merely unwired: `optimiser.toon.measuresavings` reports wrong percentages and divides by zero (X7 finding), and `llmlingua.isavailable` returned TRUE for an ABSENT sidecar until the T-31 sweep, so no prompt had ever been compressed even where the call existed. Wiring it before those are settled would wire a fail-open. Recorded so the marker is not flipped on a commit message. |
| GA1.5a | M | Blocked — needs the decision between (a) and (b) below | **A pre-flight cost estimate needs a model, and the budget is checked before one is chosen** — `forward()` now passes a real estimated TOKEN count to `budget.check` (`t.esttokens` over the system prompt plus the anonymised text), and `calccostnano` exists, so the arithmetic is no longer the obstacle. The estimated COST is still `0.0`, because cost per token depends on the model and `rtr.routerequest` runs after the budget check. Two ways to close it, and the choice is a decision about what a budget ceiling MEANS rather than a refactor: (a) check the budget after routing — routing spends nothing, so the reorder is free, but a request is then routed before being refused; (b) estimate against the most expensive ELIGIBLE model, which is the conservative reading for a guard and never admits a request that could breach the ceiling. The same question applies to `policy.classifyrisk`'s `estimatedcost` parameter in `decide()` and `risktierof()`, which also pass `0.0` — so until this lands, every governance decision in browser mode is taken as though the request were free. Filed rather than guessed at in passing. **Premise corrected 2026-09-24:** the first sentence above is not true of this tree. At 77c08d6 `forward()` calls `budget.check(budgetmanager(store);0 as u32;0.0)` — the token estimate is ZERO too, not `t.esttokens`. `shared.types.esttokens` exists and `core.memory.context` uses it, but nothing on the request path does, and no commit's `pipeline.tk` ever called it. So a pre-flight check today refuses only a budget that is ALREADY spent. Wiring the token estimate needs no decision and could land first; the cost estimate still needs (a) or (b). `classifyrisk` is still passed `0.0` at `pipeline.tk:176,182` |
| GA1.5b | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **The budget's day number is read twice and can straddle midnight** — found 2026-09-24 by not dismissing a flaky test. `browser.pages.api.pipeline.persistbudget` stores `daynumber()` (`str.nowunix()/86400`) alongside the token count, and `budgetmanager` later compares the stored day against a FRESH `daynumber()`; if they differ it returns `budget.new(...)` with zero usage. Across midnight those two reads disagree, so the persisted usage is discarded and the day's spend silently resets to zero **mid-request**. Observed: `tests/integration/test_pipeline_api.tk`'s assertion *"a manager built after the write carries the persisted token count"* failed once in a full-suite run that crossed 00:00, and passed on every run before and after — including twice in isolation immediately afterwards. The window is one comparison wide, which is why it reads as flakiness; the consequence is that a user who exceeds a daily budget at 23:59:59 is not blocked at 00:00:00, which is arguably correct, and one who is at their limit mid-request has the limit vanish, which is not. Fix by threading ONE day number through the request — compute it once where the request starts and pass it to both — rather than calling the clock twice. A test that fakes the clock across a boundary is the only way to assert it; `t.esttokens`-style injection of a timestamp parameter is the cheap route. **Closed 2026-09-24.** The consequence stated above was wrong in its direction, corrected here: in `completed()` the manager was built with one clock read and persisted with a second, so across 00:00 the load saw day N-1, loaded yesterday's usage, added the request and stamped the sum with day N. Yesterday's whole spend carried into today (over-counting, refusing a user near yesterday's limit for the next day); nothing reset to zero. The test flake was the test's own two clock reads. Fix: `budgetmanagerat`/`persistbudgetat` take the day as a parameter, and `accumulatebudget(store;tokens;cost;day)` loads and persists against one day that `completed()` reads once. `forward()`'s pre-check keeps its own single read; a check on day N-1 and an accumulate on day N attribute the request to the day it completed, which is correct. `tests/integration/test_pipeline_api.tk` seeds 500 tokens on day d0 and asserts a request on d0+1 leaves 10, then 25; mutation (load with `day-1`, i.e. the straddle) fails 3 assertions. The earlier assertions now use the injected day too |
| GA1.6 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **Wire semantic cache into pipeline** — Before dispatching to LLM, check `core.optimiser.cache.lookup()` for semantically similar previous requests. If cache hit (similarity > 0.92), return cached response with `[cache hit]` indicator. On cache miss, store response via `cache.store()` after receiving. Target: up to 73% savings on cache hits per spec.<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-23.** `forward()` now looks the request up in the cache before anything else is spent on it: `cachelookup(run;res.anonymised)` then `cache.ishit(hit)`, returning `cachedreply` on a hit. The blocker this row did not know about was the TYPE: `$cacheresult{$none:i64;$some:i64}` could not hold a response — `lookup` always constructed `$some:0`, so a hit returned the literal zero. The live path uses `$cachehit` plus an `ishit()` predicate, which is the sentinel-struct idiom T-18 and T-31 force on every result type; `$cacheresult` survives only for the two result-returning wrappers, documented at `cache.tk:33`. Asserted in `tests/unit/optimiser/test_optimiser_outcomes.tk`. |
| GA1.7 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23, merged with X8.6 | **Wire intelligent router into pipeline** — Replace the if/else API key check with actual `core.router.selector.select()` which evaluates sensitivity × cost × latency × capability to choose the optimal model. Currently the router makes simplistic binary local/cloud choices; the spec requires multi-dimensional optimisation.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Reopened** — `selector.select()` is never called; the provider is chosen by `str.len(anthropickey)>20`. Overlaps X8.6, which owns the four named strategies<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-23.** Merged with X8.6 as the plan prescribed, because they are the same code. `core/router/strategy.tk` (new) implements the four strategies: `cheapestadequate`, `fastest`, `bestquality`, `localfirst`, each with its own comparator over `$modelcapability` and a shared `eligible` filter. `forward()` calls `rtr.routerequest(...)` and tests `rtr.isrouted(route)`; the provider is no longer chosen by `str.len(anthropickey)>20`. `routerequest` returns a PLAIN `$routingdecision`, not `!$lokeerr` — a failed selection read as a success picks a garbage provider, and for sensitivity-scored routing that means potentially sending restricted data to a cloud endpoint, which is the worst failure available and would have been introduced by the story meant to prevent it. The RESTRICTED property is checked twice on purpose, inside `routerequest` and again in `forward` before dispatch; `tests/unit/router/test_route_safety.tk` (new, 210 lines) drives it under a hostile configuration by three independent routes with a PUBLIC control that IS cloud-routed, so the test can fail in both directions. Decision **R** — the patent read before the router is promoted outwardly — is unaffected and still open. |
| GA1.8 | S | **Done** | **Wire governance policy evaluation** — Before dispatching, call `core.governance.policy.evaluate()` to get risk classification (low/medium/high) and decision ($allow/$allowwithwarning/$requireapproval). Show warnings to user per graduated severity. Currently policy evaluation exists but is never called from the browser.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done — 2026-09-23.** Recovered under GA1.10, and the cheapest of the GA1 wirings for a structural reason: `policy.evaluate` returns a **plain struct**, so T-18 cannot touch it, and `classifyrisk` takes its cost as an f64 **parameter**, which crosses a module boundary intact. A policy deny is audited before the 403 is returned — a blocked request is exactly the kind an auditor asks about |

Document the verified flow.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Reopened** — cannot pass while GA1.5-GA1.7 are unwired, and the existing test asserts on JSON shape rather than on whether anonymisation occurred *Done 2026-09-23. `tests/integration/test_request_path_e2e.tk`, 20 assertions, driving the ROUTE'S OWN exported functions rather than re-implementing the sequence — `h.decide`, `h.isdeny`, `h.risktierof`, `h.restoretext`, `h.recordevent`, `h.killswitchengaged` — so a regression in any of them fails this test. It composes them rather than calling `post(req)` because toke cannot construct an http request from source (T-16): `std.http` exposes `reqpath`/`reqmethod`/`reqbody` as accessors over an opaque `i64` and no constructor, so `post` is unreachable from a test. At `2b8942e`, `packages/browser/pages/api/pipeline.tk` was **240 lines** and really did call `ph.newmap()` (`:85`), `ph.addentry()` (`:93`), `ks.isengaged()` (`:116`), `policy.classifyrisk()`/`policy.evaluate()` (`:137-138`), `ph.restore()` (`:195`) and `audit.logevent()` (`:217`). `09b0a67` — *"fix(F10.18): ooke.template.renderfile -> tplrenderfile"*, a mechanical 24-site sweep — cut the file to **79 lines**, net **-161**, keeping the imports and deleting every call. Its commit message does not mention the file, and it is the **only** file in that commit that shrank by more than 30 lines, so this was collateral, not intent. `docs/claims.md` had recorded the hazard in advance: the working-tree copy *"strips all nine core imports and must not be committed (F10.13, X8.5)"*. It was committed anyway. **So `2b8942e` is the reference implementation for GA1.2/GA1.4/GA1.8, not prose** — recover from it rather than writing fresh, but it needs adapting: the pipeline API changed with NC1.9 (`runforward` + `issafetoforward`), and the recovered `ph.addentry` path still depends on PL1.1, because the placeholder map it built is the one `regex.rundetection` throws away |

## Epic GA2: Security Hardening

*API keys stored in plaintext JSON, SQLite unencrypted, no license headers. These are specification requirements that are not met.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| GA2.1 | L | **Reopened** — keys already in settings.json are never migrated, and were the only keys read | **Migrate API keys to OS keychain** — Replace `~/.loke/settings.json` plaintext key storage with OS keychain integration. On macOS use Security framework via toke/ooke bindings. Keys fetched per-request with expiry validation, never written to disk, never appear in logs. Update `settings.tk` handler and all consumers. Spec: "Secrets and API keys stored in OS keychain, NEVER in config files." **Reopened 2026-09-24 (RV1.4).** "Never written to disk" is false in practice. Saving a key through `/api/settings` moves it to the keychain and removes it from `settings.json`, and that part works. **Nothing migrates a key that is already in `settings.json`.** Measured on the development machine: both provider keys were still there in plaintext (only their presence and length were read, not their values). Until X4.7 the request path read ONLY `settings.json`, so the keychain was never consulted for a request. "Fetched per-request with expiry validation" has no implementation. Remaining work: a one-time migration on first read, which moves the key to the keychain and removes it from the file, with a test using a temporary settings path. Also a decision on expiry validation, which no provider key actually carries. Claim A9 is corrected to `partial`. |
| GA2.2 | M | **Reopened** | **Enable SQLCipher encryption** — Switch from plaintext SQLite to SQLCipher for `~/.loke/loke.db`. Derive encryption key from OS keychain. All tables encrypted at rest. Spec: "All tables encrypted at rest via SQLCipher from OS keychain secret."<br><br>**Status detail:** **Reopened — see X8.1.** Not done: `PRAGMA key` is issued and silently ignored by plain SQLite, so the database is plaintext on disk. SQLCipher is not available in the toolchain and may never be; the replacement approach is field-level `std.encrypt` under X8.2 |
| GA2.3 | L | **Done** | **Add Apache 2.0 license headers to all source files** — Add `// Copyright 2026 loke contributors\n// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` header to all 629 `.tk` files and all `.tkt` template files (using appropriate comment syntax). Spec: "ALL source files MUST include header at top." Currently 0% compliance. |
| GA2.4 | S | **Done** | **Auto-redact PII from all log output** — Implement log redaction filter per spec: "ALL log output passes through auto-redaction filter. Debug mode subject to same redaction rules as production." Ensure no PII appears in any log, console output, or error message. **Re-verified 2026-09-24 (RV1.4), against the running server.** Two POSTs to `/api/pipeline` carried a unique synthetic email and a test card number. The first was scanned and forwarded; the second tried to route to a cloud model. Afterwards neither value appeared in the server's stdout or stderr, in `~/.loke/loke.log`, or in any file under `~/.loke`, the database included (`grep -rl`). The error returned to the client names no input. Scope of this check: the forward and provider-failure paths. A successful provider response was not exercised, because no provider was reachable. |
| GA2.5 | S | Blocked — T-43: no toke API can set the bind address | **Localhost-only HTTP binding enforcement** — Verify HTTP server binds to `127.0.0.1` only (not `0.0.0.0`). If external binding configured, log as warning per spec. Verify CORS restricted to localhost origins. **Reopened 2026-09-24 (RV1.4), then blocked.** Measured with `lsof` against the running server: it listens on `*:11430`, every interface. `ooke.toml:8` sets `host = "127.0.0.1"` and nothing reads it. ooke's `serverun` takes no host, and toke's glue passes a NULL host, which means `INADDR_ANY`. No toke API at the pin can choose a bind address, so this is filed upstream as T-43. What is enforced meanwhile is per request: GA2.6 refuses a foreign browser Origin, and GA2.7 refuses any non-loopback Host. A non-browser client on the same network can still send `Host: 127.0.0.1` and reach the API. The CORS half of this row holds (verified under RV1.2). Claim K7 is corrected from `verified` to `stub`. |
| GA2.6 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **A hostile web page could drive loke's state-changing endpoints** — Found 2026-09-24 by RV1.2 while checking CORS. CORS was correct, and it is the wrong control for this. It stops a page READING a response, not SENDING a request, and a POST with `Content-Type: text/plain` needs no preflight. Measured: a POST to `/api/pipeline` with `Origin: https://evil.example` was parsed, scanned and forwarded to the provider, and failed only because none was running. So any site open in the user's browser could spend their API budget and write audit rows, and through `/api/settings` it could change what loke talks to. **Fixed:** `shared.origin` refuses any request whose Origin header is not http on a loopback host. That is enforced first in all six POST handlers: approve, pipeline, privacy, savings, settings and tabs. A request with NO Origin, which is curl, the CLI or a test, is allowed, and `null` is refused. **Evidence:** `tests/unit/shared/test_origin.tk` has 12 assertions, including three prefix tricks such as `http://127.0.0.1.evil.example`. With the refusal disabled, 7 of them failed. `scripts/smoke_serve.sh` asserts 403 for a hostile origin on all six routes and that a loopback origin still gets through, against the running server. **DNS rebinding:** a rebound POST is also refused, because its Origin names the attacker's host rather than a loopback one. **Not covered:** a rebound page can still READ GET routes as same-origin, such as `/api/settings`, which shows provider keys masked to four characters. A Host-header check would close that, and it is filed as GA2.7 |
| GA2.7 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **Refuse requests whose Host header is not loopback, to close DNS-rebinding reads** — Filed 2026-09-24 from GA2.6. A page on an attacker's name that resolves to 127.0.0.1 is same-origin with loke as far as the browser knows, so it can READ any GET route: `/api/settings` (provider keys masked to four characters, and the provider URLs), `/api/savings` and `/api/health`. GA2.6 already refuses rebound POSTs by Origin. The fix is a Host check in front of every route, accepting only `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` and `[::1]`, with or without a port. It belongs in ooke's dispatch, or in a wrapper every handler calls. `scripts/smoke_serve.sh` should assert 403 for `Host: rebind.example` **Done 2026-09-24.** `shared.origin.hostrefused` accepts a missing Host or `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]` with an optional port. `requestrefused` combines it with GA2.6's Origin rule and is the first statement of all 13 API handler functions, GET and POST, across eight files. HTML pages are not guarded: they carry no data of their own and fetch everything from these routes. **Evidence:** 8 Host assertions in `tests/unit/shared/test_origin.tk`, of which 3 failed with the rule disabled. `scripts/smoke_serve.sh` asserts that `GET /api/settings` and `/api/health` with `Host: rebind.example` get 403 from the running server. |

## Epic GA3: UX Alignment with Design Principles

*Accessibility, graduated warnings, progressive disclosure, and cost pre-estimation are specified in design principles but weakly or not implemented.*

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| GA3.1 | L | **Done** — 2026-09-25 | **Accessibility overhaul** — Add ARIA labels to all interactive elements across all templates. Include: `aria-expanded` on collapsible panels, `aria-label` on icon buttons (thumbs, settings, nav), `role="dialog"` on modals, `aria-live="polite"` on dynamic content (typing indicator, pipeline console, loading states), skip navigation links, visible focus states, logical tab order. Currently only 2 ARIA labels in the entire app. Score: 2/10. **Re-verified by reading 2026-09-24 (RV1.4).** `aria-live` appears in seven moke templates, `role="dialog"` in `base.tkt` and `chat.tkt`, and icon buttons carry `aria-label`. Tab order, visible focus and skip links need a browser. It has never been exercised: these features live in `packages/moke/templates/chat.tkt`, moke has no binary (MK21.16), so the page cannot be served, and no test drives it. It is Partial under the legend rule that a runtime **Done** names the evidence that exercised it. It closes when moke links and the behaviour is checked in a browser. **Exercised 2026-09-25.** Run in a real browser by `scripts/smoke_ui.sh`: headless Chrome, driven over the DevTools protocol by `scripts/lib/ui_check.mjs`, against moke, loke and a recording fake model, all on throwaway HOMEs. The checks are in `tests/ui/chat_ga3.json`, and the script is in the gate's test step. Passing: `aria-live` regions, `role=dialog`, an accessible name on every button, a skip-navigation link, `aria-expanded` on collapsible panels, and a `:focus-visible` outline in the loaded styles. **Not asserted:** logical tab order, which needs a keyboard walk through the page. **Tab order added 2026-09-25, so every item in this row is now asserted in a browser.** No element sets a positive `tabindex`. With real Tab key presses sent through the browser's input pipeline, the first Tab lands on the skip link and the next moves forward in document order. |
| GA3.2 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-25 | **Graduated warning system** — Implement 4-level warning system per spec: Info (non-interruptive, shown in pipeline view), Advisory (subtle indicator, dismissible), Warning (clear banner, requires acknowledgement), Block (cannot proceed without decision). Currently all alerts display at the same level. Apply to: PII detection, cost thresholds, sensitivity changes, policy violations. **Reopened 2026-09-24 (RV1.4), by reading `packages/moke/templates/chat.tkt`.** (1) `showWarning` is called with four levels, but its `if` and `else` branches render the same dismiss button. So "warning (requires acknowledgement)" and "block (cannot proceed without decision)" are both dismissable banners, and neither gates anything. The code also uses `caution` where the spec says `warning`. (2) The RESTRICTED-data warning ("review before sending") is raised inside the RESPONSE handler, so it appears after the text has been sent. The user is asked to review something that has already left. The kill-switch `block` does stop the request, but that stop is the kill-switch check, not the warning level. To close: an acknowledgement step for warning, a decision gate for block, and the RESTRICTED check moved before dispatch. Moving it can use the pipeline's dry run (`rundry`), which scans without sending. It has never been exercised: these features live in `packages/moke/templates/chat.tkt`, moke has no binary (MK21.16), so the page cannot be served, and no test drives it. It is Partial under the legend rule that a runtime **Done** names the evidence that exercised it. It closes when moke links and the behaviour is checked in a browser. **Fixed and exercised 2026-09-25.** `showWarning` now distinguishes the levels. `info` and `advisory` are dismissable and expire. `warning` has no dismiss button, does not expire, and clears only on "I understand". `block` cannot be dismissed. The old `caution` maps to `warning`. The post-reply RESTRICTED banner no longer says "review before sending": it states that the prompt was anonymised before it left, and points to the pipeline view. The review before sending is the A4.4 confirmation step, which already intercepts sensitive prompts before dispatch. Run in a real browser by `scripts/smoke_ui.sh`: headless Chrome, driven over the DevTools protocol by `scripts/lib/ui_check.mjs`, against moke, loke and a recording fake model, all on throwaway HOMEs. The checks are in `tests/ui/chat_ga3.json`, and the script is in the gate's test step. The info banner is watched expiring after about 5 s, and the warning is watched staying past that. With the warning branch restored to a plain dismiss button, the warning and caution checks FAILED. |
