Build clean, 224 modules |
| GA5.15 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **`cost_usd` must become an exact integer number of cents, and the change must be atomic** — Found 2026-09-23 by GA1.9's end-to-end test: `cost_usd` is REAL, so **SQLite owns its rendering**. `str.fromfloat(0.0)` produces `"0"` and the column gives back `"0.0"`, so the writer hashed one string and the verifier recomputed over another, and **an untampered chain failed on every row the request path writes** — because the cost estimate is deliberately 0.0 until LS1.1h. `0.005` happened to survive; `0.0` did not. **Mitigated for now** by normalising both sides through the same integer (`f64hundredths` writing, `rowf64hundredths` verifying), which is stable whatever SQLite does with a REAL, at the stated cost that the digest covers cost only to the nearest hundredth. **My first design for the full fix was wrong and was corrected during review — recorded because the reasoning is the useful part.** I claimed the canonical form already worked against an INTEGER-cents column. It does not; it works only for zero. `rowf64hundredths` reads the column as text and multiplies by 100, i.e. it treats the column as **dollars** — so against a cents column a stored `1` recomputes as `"100"` against a hashed `"1"`. My test agreed only because every row the request path writes today has cost 0.0, **the same coincidence that hid the original defect.** Three further problems, all real: changing the DDL alone creates a 100× units bug, since the writer still sends dollars while `dashboard`'s `SUM(cost_usd)` assumes dollars and `eventsfromrows` divides by 100 — and that figure feeds a spending quota; the type alone does not restore precision, because it is lost on the READ side by `rowf64hundredths`, so the verifier must use `rowi64`; and SQLite cannot change a column type in place, needing CREATE-new / INSERT-SELECT / DROP / RENAME — a wholesale rewrite of every row of an append-only tamper-evident trail, which is what GA5.9 exists to prevent. **Agreed design:** rename the column `cost_usd_cents` so the units cannot be misread and every stale reader fails loudly rather than silently by 100×; the writer stores exact integer cents; the verifier reads with `rowi64`. All three in one change, `audit.tk` and the migration together. The general lesson stands: **a digest over a value whose text representation the database controls is a digest over the database's formatting choices**. **Closed 2026-09-24, as the agreed design, in one commit.** Migration 27 renames `cost_usd` to `cost_usd_legacy_dollars` (RENAME COLUMN rewrites no row, so the append-only triggers are not engaged) and migration 28 adds `cost_usd_cents INTEGER`; the writer stores `f64hundredths(cost)`, the same integer it hashes; the verifier reads it with `rowi64`. The part the design left open: pre-migration rows are not rewritten (GA5.9), so `core.storage.db.rowauditcostcents` reads a row whose cents are NULL through the legacy column with the rounding its digest was written with — the digest string is the same integer in both eras, so old chains still verify. Every reader moved in the same change: `audit.tk` (insert, verify, `eventsfromrows`, `getrecent`), `dashboard.tk` and `scorecard.tk` sums (via `auditcostcentsexpr`, `scalari64`), `report-engine.tk`. `tests/unit/storage/test_audit_cost_cents.tk`: `typeof` is integer, $1.50 stored as 150, $0.29 as 29, `SELECT cost_usd` errors, the chain verifies over 0/150/29 and across a hand-built legacy row, the spend total and the provider scorecard both read 204, and a one-cent edit breaks the chain. Mutation (writer sends dollars into the cents column, the 100x bug predicted above) fails 9 assertions. Precision is a cent, now a property of the stored value rather than a gap between stored and hashed |
| GA5.16 | M | **Done** | **`governance/scorecard.tk` publishes invented figures** — Found 2026-09-23. `buildscorecard(store:i64)` **ignores its store** and returns hardcoded statistics: anthropic 89 requests, openai 34, ollama 19, with invented latencies (`p99latencyms:890`), error rates and costs (`avgcostperreq:0.31/89.0`). `formatscorecard` then renders them under the heading "Provider scorecard". Anything that displays this publishes numbers nobody measured, which is the one thing `CLAUDE.md` names as a hard constraint and `docs/metrics-baseline.md` exists to prevent. Two acceptable outcomes and no third: derive every figure from `audit_log` — which now has rows (GA1.4) and the columns for provider, tokens and duration — or **delete the module and withdraw the scorecard** until it can be derived. Do not correct the numbers to different invented numbers. Sibling of DA1.5, which was the same defect on the savings page *Done 2026-09-23. Every field now DERIVED from `audit_log`: provider names read from the trail (so a provider never called does not appear — "openai" was hardcoded and appeared on installations that had never called it), request counts from `COUNT(id)`, average latency from `AVG(duration_ms)`, and p99 from `ORDER BY … LIMIT 1 OFFSET` since SQLite has no percentile function — with fewer than 100 rows that is the maximum, which is the honest answer for a small sample rather than an interpolation nobody asked for. Costs carried in integer **hundredths**, because `cost_usd` is a REAL whose rendering SQLite owns (that broke the audit digest twice) and because a bare f64 crossing a module boundary is corrupted. **`errorratepct` is REMOVED rather than zeroed** — `audit_log` has no success/failure discriminator, so any figure there would state that no request has ever failed; the column renders `n/r` for NOT RECORDED, which cannot be mistaken for a measurement the way a dash or a zero can. Filed as GA5.19. `tests/integration/test_scorecard_derived.tk`, 14 assertions, every one a **relationship** between what was written and what is reported rather than a value check — the fabrication would have passed any test that only asserted "three providers with non-zero requests", because that was true of it.* |
| GA5.17 | M | **Done** | **`monitoring.tk` repeats GA5.1 and GA5.11 in a second table** — Found 2026-09-23. `quality_signals` inserts `created_at` as the SQL **literal string** `'now()'` (line 66), so every row's timestamp is the seven characters `now()`; and line 124 filters `created_at >= datetime('now','-N days')`, the same epoch-ms-against-ISO string comparison that made every governance report window match nothing. Both fixes already exist in `audit.tk` and `db.tk` (`msrangeof`, `daysagoms`, `createdatwindowclause`) — this is applying a known pattern to a table the first pass did not cover, not new design **Done 2026-09-24. The first clause was already fixed; the second has the mechanism wrong, and what is actually left runs the opposite way.** `quality_signals` now inserts `datetime('now')` unquoted, so the literal-`now()` half is closed. But this table's `created_at` has always been TEXT holding `datetime('now')` output — never an epoch-ms integer — so `created_at >= datetime('now','-N days')` is ISO against ISO, which sorts lexicographically and is a **correct** window needing none of `msrangeof` / `daysagoms` / `createdatwindowclause`. The row's \"same epoch-ms-against-ISO comparison that made every governance report window match nothing\" does not apply here.<br><br>**The residual the row missed:** rows written before the writer was fixed hold the five characters `now()`, and lexicographically `'n'` (0x6E) exceeds `'2'` (0x32) — so `\"now()\" >= <any ISO bound>` is TRUE and every such row appears in EVERY window for ever, carrying a timestamp that means nothing. A defect that admits stale rows to every period is quieter than one that admits none, because the figures stay plausible. Fixed with a shape test, `created_at LIKE '____-__-__%'`, in `monitoring.computetrend` and in `feedback.store.getsummary`, which has the identical history and the identical residual. |
| GA5.18 | S | **Done** | **`std.db.exec` silently applies only the FIRST statement of a multi-statement string** — Found 2026-09-23 while writing the GA5.9 triggers. It is `sqlite3_prepare_v2` plus step, and it **discards the tail pointer**, so a two-statement migration applies the first and reports success for both. Documented in `migrations.tk` and worked around there by one statement per migration. Nothing scans for it: grep every `db.exec` call in the repo for a `;` inside the SQL. Also worth an upstream note — a binding that accepts a multi-statement string and silently runs one of them is the T-23 family, an interface that cannot express what it does **Done 2026-09-24.** `scripts/find_multistatement_exec.py`, wired into `quality-gate.sh --step checks`. It resolves four argument shapes — a string literal, `str.concat`/`str.join` nests, a same-file helper called with no arguments, and a local `let sql=` — and then two indirections the first version was blind to: calls to PASS-THROUGH wrappers (`core.storage.db.exec`/`execparams` and same-file equivalents taking `sql:str`), which is the layer most callers actually use, and any `sql:` field initialiser, which is where `db.exec(mg.sql;…)` reads from. Ignores a trailing `;`, a `;` inside an SQL string or `--` comment, and `proc.exec`. Demonstrated FAILING against a fixture for every shape before being trusted: 0 real sites in `packages` and `tests`, 21 unresolvable ones reported with `--verbose`. The upstream note this row also asks for is filed in `docs/upstream/toke-issues.md`. |
| GA5.19 | S | **Done** | **`audit_log` has no success/failure discriminator, so no error rate is derivable** — Found 2026-09-23 while deriving the scorecard (GA5.16). Every row carries `event_type='llm_request'`, including the ones the request path writes when a provider call **fails** — `providerfailed` audits the 502 path, correctly, but with the same event type. So there is no way to compute an error rate, and reporting 0% would state that no request has ever failed. The scorecard now renders `n/r` for NOT RECORDED. The fix is a value, not a schema change: write `llm_request_failed` from the failure path. `event_type` is already covered by the chain digest, so no migration and no digest decision is involved — which is why this is S. It does mean existing rows are all indistinguishable, and that should be said rather than back-filled **Done 2026-09-24.** `recordevent` takes the event type as a parameter and the eight call sites in `browser/pages/api/pipeline.tk` write five values: `llm_request`, `llm_request_failed`, `llm_request_refused`, `quota_exceeded`, `policy_deny`. The last two are the names `core.governance.report:135,141` was already querying for and never finding, so two long-inert governance figures now have a source as well. `scorecard.tk` derives `errors` and `errorratebp` — basis points, because an f64 percentage is corrupted crossing to the formatter (T-20) — and `errorsrecorded` now means \"this provider has attempts, so the rate is a measurement\" rather than \"no source exists\". **A second defect this row did not name:** every per-provider figure was computed over every row carrying that provider name, and the refusal paths write a real provider and model id with a duration and no cost — so a provider this endpoint has no client for was counted as serving requests quickly and for free, and the rows carrying provider `'none'` made `'none'` a provider in the scorecard. One `dispatchclause()` restricts all five queries. Asserted in `test_scorecard.tk` through the figures a 7000ms refusal would distort. |
| GA5.20 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **`db.countquery` returns a row count and reads as a scalar reader** — Found 2026-09-23 by misusing it. It is `db.many(sql).len`, so `SELECT COUNT(id) AS cnt FROM …` returns **1** for any non-empty table, not the count. Its comment says it is for existence checks and `tableexists`/`triggerexists` use it correctly, but the name invites the other reading and the failure is silent: every provider reported exactly one request until an assertion comparing the count against the number of rows actually written caught it. Either rename it (`rowsreturned`) or add a scalar sibling (`scalarquery(sql;params;col)` over `db.one` + `rowi64`) — the second is more useful, since reading a single aggregate is common enough that four call sites already hand-roll it<br><br>**Done 2026-09-24. There is no live misuse; the trap is closed rather than a bug fixed.** `countquery` returns `rows.len`, which is exactly right for its three callers — `tableexists`, `triggerexists` and `placeholder_store.hasrequest` all ask *is there a row?*. Handed an aggregate it answers **1** for every input, because a COUNT returns ONE ROW containing the count and this function measures the row list. Swept first: **no call site in `packages/` or `src/` passes it an aggregate**, and the aggregate readers all use `db.many`/`db.one` and read their named column correctly. But it is a plausible thing to reach for — the name contains "count" — and it caught this session once, reporting every provider as having made exactly 1 request. So `core.storage.db.scalari64(sql;params;col;fallback)` is the reader it gets mistaken for: the column name is explicit rather than positional, so a second aggregate added to the same query cannot silently return the wrong one, and the fallback is the caller's rather than a hardcoded zero — for a count 0 is right, for "the highest version applied" 0 and "no rows" mean different things. `countquery`'s own comment now carries the trap where the next person will read it. |
| GA5.22 | M |  | **`quota.check` discards the tokens and cost it is asked about, so it cannot pre-authorise** — `check(store;scope;tokens;cost)` takes the size of the request being considered and uses neither; it reports the quota's CURRENT state. Measured 2026-09-24: `check(store;"team-a";5000;0.0)` against a 1000-token quota with nothing recorded returns `exceeded=false, usedtokens=0` — it would authorise a request five times the whole allowance. So a caller can only spend first and ask afterwards, which is not what a quota is for. Latent: nothing calls `check` today (grep over `packages/` and `src/`). **Filed rather than fixed, deliberately.** The fix changes what `exceeded` MEANS — from *"am I over?"* to *"would this take me over?"* — and both readings have callers who would be right to expect them (a dashboard wants the first, a gate wants the second). Decide whether `check` gains the pre-authorisation reading or a second function does, then thread the pending amounts into `computequotastatusrow` and handle the no-usage-row case, which today returns 100% remaining and would authorise a first request of any size. |
| GA5.23 | S |  | **Quota fails OPEN, unlike every other control in this codebase** — measured 2026-09-24: an unknown scope is UNLIMITED, and a rule whose limits are both zero is UNLIMITED — 999999 tokens against `limittokens:0` returns `exceeded=false, remaining=100%`. Both directions are now asserted in `tests/unit/governance/test_quota.tk` so the behaviour is explicit rather than incidental. That is the opposite of how this project treats an unknown state everywhere else: the kill switch treats an unreadable flag as ENGAGED, the privacy pipeline refuses with 503 when no detection layer completed (NC1.9), and `memory.decay` refuses to sweep a row whose age it cannot read. **Filed rather than flipped, because flipping it could lock a user out of their own tool** on a fresh installation with no rules configured — which is exactly the state a first run is in. The decision is whether a zero limit means "no limit" or "nothing allowed", and whether an unknown scope inherits a default rule. Neither is a defect with an obvious correct answer, and a governance default is not something to change in passing. |

Migrations 24-26. The sweep took three attempts to become usable and the failures are recorded in its docstring: raw text matched English prose in comments (76 hits, 71 of them words like "from another"); scanning only string literals still matched user-facing messages (33); requiring UPPERCASE SQL keywords and a SQL verb in the literal got it to 6, of which 3 were real. `tabs` is allowlisted — `browser/src/tabs.tk` is in the check-only `browser-app` target, blocked on `std.webview`, and inventing a schema for a tree nobody can exercise would be guessing. |
| GA5.25 | M | Blocked — needs a product decision on purposes and on block-or-warn | **Consent is recorded by a module nothing calls, so no consent is ever captured** — Found 2026-09-24 while closing GA5.7. `core.governance.consent` is complete and works: `grant`, `revoke`, `check` and `loadprefs` over a `consent_records` table with a `UNIQUE(user_id, purpose)` constraint, and `tests/unit/governance/test_consent.tk` exercises it. **It has no caller anywhere in the product** — no route, no CLI command, no browser page invokes `grant`, so `consent_records` is empty on every installation and `compliance.checkconsentrecorded` correctly reports WARN for ever. That WARN is not a defect and must not be made to say PASS: it is an accurate report of an absent feature, and GA5.7's whole point is that a status must be derived rather than asserted. What is missing is the capture surface — where a user is asked, which purposes are offered, and what happens to a request whose purpose has no consent. The last of those is the part with teeth: `consent.check` exists and nothing on the request path consults it, so a purpose the user has explicitly REVOKED is not currently a reason to refuse anything. Needs a product decision on the purpose list and on whether a missing consent blocks or warns, then a surface and a gate. Blocks the consent clause of GA5.7 and nothing else. **Options recorded 2026-09-24, not chosen.** The purposes are already enumerated by `$consentpurposes` in `consent.tk`: `telemetry`, `improvement`, `cloudrouting`, `companionsync`. Only `cloudrouting` bears on the request path. A gate needs three decisions: (1) the SUBJECT — the request path carries no user identifier, so a single local subject id is itself a choice; (2) on missing or revoked `cloudrouting` consent, either (a) refuse cloud and route local-only, which fails closed and matches "the user is the authority", or (b) warn and proceed, which keeps today's behaviour and makes consent advisory; (3) where it is asked — first run, or the first time a request would leave the device. No code written: each option changes what a user sees, and inventing one would be the decision this row says is missing |

| GA5.28 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-25 | **Every clock read through `str.nowunix()` was a heap address: kill-switch expiry, the budget day, request durations and metrics windows** — Found 2026-09-24 by the disclosure worker, which diagnosed the intermittent `test_kill_switch` failure before it hit a usage limit. Confirmed and widened on 2026-09-25. Measured: `str.fromint(str.nowunix())` is 37631297824, then 37631297856 on the next call, while `let s:str=str.nowunix()` reads 1790327645. The function returns a string and is declared `i64` (T-36, T-41). **17 sites did arithmetic on it:** `kill_switch.engage` and `checkautorelease` (whether a timed block had expired depended on the allocator); `pipeline.daynumber` (the budget's "today" was an address divided by 86400); every `duration_ms` the request path writes to the audit trail, and the `metrics_raw` timestamp; and the four metrics windows in `core.audit.metrics`. **Fixed:** seconds come from `shared.types.nowi64()` and milliseconds from `nowms()`. The tests that had used the same address clock were moved to the real one. **Evidence:** a real-clock assertion on `engaged_at` in `test_kill_switch.tk`, one on `daynumber` in `test_pipeline_handler.tk`, and `test_metrics_dispatch.tk`. With the three modules reverted, those failed 1, 1 and 19 assertions respectively. `test_audit_metrics.tk` stayed green either way, so it does not pin this. **Guarded** by `scripts/find_nowunix_as_int.py` in the gate, which flags every code use; the old kill switch has 2, and there are 0 now. |
| GA5.29 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-26 | **The running server stored nothing on a fresh install: no audit row, decision trace, budget or metrics** — Found 2026-09-26 by DA1.7, whose fail-closed reserve refused every request in `smoke_e2e.sh`. `core.storage.db.openstore` never created its data directory. With a fresh HOME, `~/.loke` did not exist when the first request opened the store, and `sqlite3_open` returned `SQLITE_CANTOPEN` (14). This was measured by interposing the sqlite calls. The `$ok` arm of `db.open` was taken anyway, and every statement after it, all thirty migrations included, ran on a NULL handle (`SQLITE_MISUSE`). The directory appeared only when the log writer created it later. No test saw it: every storage test creates its directory first, and no smoke test read the server's database. So GA5.5's trace, DA1.3's metrics and the audit trail were all asserted against test stores, never against the server. **Fixed:** `openstore` creates the directory, then checks the connection with `SELECT 1` and returns an error if it does not answer. **Evidence:** `tests/unit/storage/test_store_fresh_dir.tk` opens a store in a directory that does not exist, and asserts the file, all migrations and a write. It fails 3 assertions with the `mkdir` removed. `smoke_e2e.sh` now reads the server's `disclosure_log` from a fresh HOME. **Not fixed:** `db.open` reporting success on failure is a toke-level defect. The liveness check covers it here, and `palace.tk` opens its own database without one. |

## Epic X8: Security Guarantee Hardening

*The controls that documents describe as protections and that do not operate. Cited in thirteen files
before this epic existed, which is why it is written now.*

> **Unblocked 2026-09-20.** `std.encrypt` is a toke library and the toolchain is available, so this epic
> is live. X8.5 stays held behind NC1.9 because `pipeline.tk` must not be edited until the fail-open is
> fixed. The stories still queue behind a compiling build — see F10.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| X8.1 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **Reverse the GA2.2 marking, and delete the false control rather than repair it** — GA2.2 was Done for SQLCipher encryption the toolchain cannot do. Done: `applyencryptionkey` and its call in `openstore` are **removed**, replaced by `s.dbencryptionavailable():bool` and `s.dbencryptionstatus():str` in `core.storage.db`, which every assertion about encryption now reads. **Deleting rather than repairing was the decision, and it is the point of the story:** the PRAGMA string was malformed (`PRAGMA key='x''<hex>''`, quotes unbalanced) AND the call discarded its own result on a user-sum error side, so T-18 meant the failure could never surface. Repairing the quoting would have made plain SQLite accept `PRAGMA key`, ignore it, and report success — converting a latent honest failure into a control indistinguishable from real encryption from inside the program, which is exactly the state GA2.2 was marked Done in. `ensuredbkey()` is still called: the key is real and X8.2 will use it for field-level encryption. Three defects, not the two first recorded |
| X8.2 | L |  | **Encrypt the mapping table at rest** — The placeholder↔value mapping is the highest-value data in the system (`docs/threat-model.md:181` rates it Maximum) and is plaintext on disk. SQLCipher is not available and may never be: toke links plain SQLite and there is no sqlcipher anywhere in the toolchain. Use field-level encryption via `std.encrypt` (`aes256gcmencrypt`/`decrypt`/`keygen`/`noncegen`, `hkdfsha` — verified present) with a per-record nonce, applied in `packages/core/src/privacy/placeholder_store.tk`. Do not reinstate a database-level claim. |
| X8.3 | M |  | **Keychain-only key handling** — The key is derived through the OS keychain and exists nowhere else: no key file, no environment-variable fallback, no default. A missing key fails the operation rather than falling back to plaintext. Test that removing the keychain entry makes the store unreadable rather than transparently readable. |
| X8.4 | M |  | **Verifiable deletion** — "Delete everything" is proven by filesystem inspection, not asserted in the interface: after deletion, no file under `~/.loke` contains any mapping value or imported record, verified by a test that greps the tree for known fixture values. Applies to MK21's workspace as well as the mapping table. |
| X8.5 | S | **Done** | **Remove the two hardcoded home directories** — `packages/browser/pages/api/pipeline.tk` and `settings.tk` both hardcode the original developer's home path, so the settings file silently resolves nowhere for anyone else. Tolerated in `scripts/known-defects.txt:20-21`; both lines come out when this lands.<br><br>**Status detail:** **Released.** Its hold cited NC1.9, which is Done. `pipeline.tk` now uses `std.homedir()` and is committed |
| X8.6 | L | **Done** — 2026-09-23, implemented (see GA1.7) | **Router strategies: implement or withdraw** — `docs/claims.md` D3: "cheapest-adequate, fastest, best-quality, local-first" has zero matches across `packages/` and `src/`. One fixed selection path exists in `packages/core/src/router/selector.tk`. F5.3 is marked **Done** and must be reopened. Either implement the four named strategies as selectable and configurable, or remove the names from `docs/features-loke.md:86`. Note decision **R** in the open-decisions register: sensitivity-scored provider routing is claimed by US 12,556,533 and wants a professional read before the router is promoted outwardly.<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-23.** Implemented rather than withdrawn: `core/router/strategy.tk` carries all four named strategies. `docs/claims.md` D3 is updated from the code. |
Related and deliberately **not** duplicated here: fail-closed on detection unavailability is **NC1.9**;
the ten orphaned multi-layer modules including `consensus` are **AD1.7**; the audit chain is **GA5.2**
and **GA5.3**.

## Epic X7: Test Suite De-stubbing

*Fifty-four test files declare the module they are meant to be testing instead of importing it. The
reason that was tolerated no longer exists.*

> **Why now:** the stub pattern existed because the linker gap made cross-module imports fail. That gap
> closed upstream — `UPSTREAM.md:77` records the verification. The excuse is dead; the stubs are not.
>
> **Unblocked 2026-09-20.** The toolchain is available, so execution queues behind the green build
> rather than upstream: a de-stubbed test has to compile to be worth anything. X7.1 needs nothing and is
> overdue.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| X7.1 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23, inventory committed to `docs/test-coverage.md` | **Inventory and triage the 95** — Re-measured 2026-09-23. This row said 54 of 86. Discovery finds **121** test files (88 under `tests/` plus **33 under `packages/moke/tests/`**), so both the numerator and the denominator were stale. **62 of 88** under `tests/` import no production module, and **33 of 33** moke tests import nothing at all — so 27% of the "122 passing" suite compiles against nothing and cannot fail. Only **26** of 121 import any production module, reaching **21** distinct `core.*` modules. Triage each: de-stub / delete / keep-with-reason. Two traps: `tests/unit/privacy/test_patterns.tk` is a genuinely good test whose asserts are wrapped in `matches`/`rejects` helpers at lines 21/25, so a raw `assert(` count flags it wrongly; and moke's 33 belong to the moke onboarding wave, not after it — a test that compiles against nothing tells you nothing about whether moke built<br><br>**Status detail — done 2026-09-23.** Re-measured a third time and the denominator moved again: discovery finds **135** test files, not 121 — other stories added tests through the day. The inventory is in [test-coverage.md](test-coverage.md) under **X7.1 — the inventory**, with a per-directory census, the list of production modules actually reached, and the files that still reach none. Both traps named in this row were real and are recorded there, plus a third: a test can be incapable of failing for a reason unrelated to stubs — `tests/unit/storage/test_settings.tk` had eleven assertions written as bare statements with the returned bool discarded, an unconditional `pass=pass+1`, and `fail` declared, never incremented, and returned. The triage also settled moke: its 33 are **untestable**, not lazy — `build/trees/moke/break-report.txt` carries 52 `COMPILE_FAIL` lines over 42 source files and every module those tests are named after is in it, while the nineteen page handlers that do compile export only `get(i64):i64` and take an ooke request handle no test can construct. 21 of the 33 make no `t.assert` call at all. |

A test that re-declares its subject cannot fail when the subject breaks, which is how A13 came to be marked complete while the browser handler posted raw text.<br><br>**Status detail — 2026-09-23.** Worked in the priority order this row names. Every stubbed file under `tests/` is de-stubbed: the suite went from 37 test files importing production code to **100 of 135**, and from 22 distinct production modules exercised to **80**. **2423** assertions now run, the great majority of them against real modules. The de-stubbing found **37** production defects, listed in [test-coverage.md](test-coverage.md). **Four were fixed by other workers the same day** — `governance.scorecard`'s COUNT aggregate, `optimiser.llmlingua`'s four inverted matches, `optimiser.profiler`'s findmin/findmax, and `router.intent`'s classification — each with a comment citing the measurement. The 33 compiled anyway — precisely BECAUSE they import nothing and assert a local struct of literals against the same literals, which is the X7.1 trap in its clearest form. Proven by `packages/moke/tests/test_upload_csv.tk` (MK21.17), **the first moke test that imports production code**: it failed to compile where the vacuous 33 passed, and now runs. That check found `/api/routing` and `/api/agents` returning invalid JSON, because 35 sites in 12 modules quoted `jx.escape`'s already-quoted output. Eight are rewritten against their modules: routing explainer, streaming, tier visualiser, cost comparison, governance, agent status, profiler and DDL. Restoring the double-quoting fails two of them. Two had no production subject at all (`pipeline_panel`, `pipeline_stages`); the per-stage record they imagined is `moke.consolelog`, which has real suites. Deleted. **Also found:** moke's generated handler registry wires only functions named `get` and `post`, so `api/ml`'s `postrun` and `postpropose`, `api/mcp`'s five handlers and `api/agents`' `postscan`, `postschema` and `getdiary` are unreachable. Nothing in the UI calls them. |
| X7.3 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-27 | **Make a stubbed test a gate failure** — Add the stub-marker check to `scripts/quality-gate.sh` with `scripts/known-defects.txt` as the shrinking allowlist, so the count can only go down.<br><br>**Status detail — 2026-09-23.** [`scripts/check_test_stubs.py`](../scripts/check_test_stubs.py) is wired into the static-checks step of [`quality-gate.sh`](../scripts/quality-gate.sh). It is enforced **per directory**, not repo-wide, and that is the point: switching it on globally only after X7.2 finishes would leave it unenforced for exactly the period in which the count could grow back. A directory joins the script's `ENFORCED` list the moment it reaches zero and never leaves; `tests/unit/storage/` was first. Three things fail a file — no live non-`std.` import, a commented-out import (the stub pattern's own signature), or a stub **declaration** marker; the marker patterns match declaration forms only and never the word in prose, because a de-stubbed test that explains what its stubs used to be must not be flagged for saying so. Proven able to fail: pointed at `tests/unit/governance/` before X7.2 ran there, it named twelve of that directory's fourteen test files and gave a reason for each. Two guards keep the list honest: a directory named in `ENFORCED` that holds no test files fails rather than passing vacuously, and the marker rule is judged at comment nesting depth 1 only, so a de-stubbed test that QUOTES the stub it removed is not flagged for doing so — reading the raw text flagged four such files before that was fixed. `scripts/known-defects.txt` is the allowlist and **carries no entries for this check**, which is the intended state — every enforced directory reached zero before enforcement began. `**Partial**` until the remaining directories are added.<br><br>**2026-09-27.** Eight more directories are enforced: `tests/unit/audit`, `companion`, `feedback`, `installer`, `mcp_toke`, `monitoring` and `pipeline` were already at zero, and `tests/unit/cli` reached zero when its two stubs were rewritten. `packages/moke/tests` is the only directory holding tests that is not enforced (33 of 40 are stubs, X7.2).<br><br>**Done 2026-09-27.** `packages/moke/tests` is enforced, so every directory that holds test files is, and a stub anywhere now fails the gate. |

## Epic LC1: Local Compute Validation on Target Hardware

*Nine stories are marked Done for behaviour that has never executed on the hardware it was written for.*

> **This epic is blocked on a purchase, not on the toolchain.** `README.md:354-360`, `docs/claims.md` §J
> and `docs/features-loke.md:161` all record the same blocker: there is no Apple Silicon test machine.
> VM1.4's containerised harness is a partial substitute and cannot validate unified memory or MLX.
> See decision **H** in the open-decisions register.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| LC1.1 | S | Blocked — hardware | **Acquire or arrange target hardware** — A base Apple Silicon Mac Mini is sufficient and is the recommendation. A cloud GPU instance cannot substitute: unified memory is the premise of the layer-offload design and a discrete-GPU instance has a PCIe boundary that predicts nothing about a Mini; MLX is Apple-Silicon-only, so a non-Apple host validates it not at all. `benchmarks/README.md:42` requires every local-inference figure to state which host produced it. |
| LC1.2 | L | Blocked — LC1.1 | **Run the five unverifiable F2 claims** — `docs/claims.md` J1-J5. Record each through `benchmarks/lib/result.py` with the host, model version, workload and N, so the results are admissible rather than anecdotal. Any that fails becomes its own defect. |
| LC1.3 | M | Blocked — LC1.1 | **MLX: exercise or withdraw** — The MLX path has zero importers and `generate()` would return `""` if called; F2.9 has no implementing code at all despite being marked Done. Either make it work on the target hardware or remove the MLX claim from the public site and the feature documents. |
| LC1.4 | M | Blocked — LC1.1 | **Validate the CPS 230 continuity control** — `docs/specifications/policy-examples/apra-cps-230.yaml:82,213` carries `validation_status: UNVALIDATED` on the local-provider fallback and states twice that an untested fallback must not be presented to an assessor as a continuity control. This is the control the whole preset rests on. Exercise the degraded path end to end with the network disabled, then change that field and nothing before. |
| LC1.5 | L | Blocked — LC1.1 | **Memory-splitting and layer offload** — Measure what actually fits and at what throughput when a model exceeds available memory, on real unified memory. This is the technique the tiered-inference design assumes; it has never been measured. Report tokens/second and resident memory per tier, and state the physical-security difference between a desk machine and a remote host in any write-up. |

## Epic LS1: Canonical Form Sweep

*toke publishes a measured pattern catalogue and a linter that enforces it. loke has never been run
through either.*

> **Why this is not bikeshedding.** `docs/guide/11-patterns-and-efficiency.md` in the toke repository
> picks one form for each everyday construct **by measurement** — the form that costs the fewest tokens
> under the decision tokenizer *and* runs as fast in as little memory as any alternative. For a project
> whose premise is reducing what is spent on a model, the canonical form is the product, not a style
> preference.
>
> Measured across 223 loke source files on 2026-09-20: **1,668 violations**.
>
> | Rule | Count | Files | Category |
> |---|---|---|---|
> **Re-measured 2026-09-23 over all 535 non-archived `.tk`.** The 2026-09-20 figures below were
> against 223 files and are stale in both directions; LS1.2–LS1.4 must be sized against these.
> The split matters because the trees outside the build cannot be gated until they compile:
>
> | rule | **built set** (`loke`) | repo-wide | category |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | `single-use-let` | 854 | **1,823** | hint |
> | `string-concat-chain` | 316 | **814** | warning |
> | `unused-import` | 196 | **306** | warning |
> | **`discarded-value-result`** | **19** | **188** | **error** |
> | `mutable-never-mutated` | 0 | 41 | warning |
> | `unused-let` / `loop-rebuilds-array` / `mut-flag-if` / `flag-soup` | 26 | 58 | mixed |
> | **total** | **1,411** | **3,230** | |
>
> Where the repo-wide error count lives: moke 87, legacy `src/` 79, built set 19, cli 3.
> `src/platform/i18n/locales.tk` alone carries **62** of the 188, in 94 lines — a file nobody has
> ever run. This is why LS1.5's lint gate must be per-tree: a repo-wide error gate would go red the
> instant moke's census is baselined and stay red through the whole onboarding.
>
> **Token efficiency here means canonical constructs, not minified source.** `toke --fmt` is the
> canonical *source* form and makes files slightly larger; `--min` is the shipped form. Do not minify
> source to chase a byte count — the catalogue's verdicts are what count.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| LS1.1 | S | **Done** — 2026-09-24, built trees at zero | **The 30 `discarded-value-result`, first and on their own** — This is a defect story, not hygiene. The rule is **error** category and its rationale is blunt: *"the discarded value is a bug in every case"*. Arrays and maps have value semantics, so a bare `arr.push(v);` or `trace.steps.push(...)` statement **returns a new collection and throws it away**, and the syntax card notes a bare push *can crash at runtime*. 30 sites in 11 files, worst `agents/observability.tk:222`. The program does not do what it says it does; fix before anything cosmetic.<br><br>**Done 2026-09-24 for the built trees, and the count was wrong in both directions.** This row said 30 `discarded-value-result`. Measured with `toke --lint` across 454 files: **61** sites repo-wide, **10** of them in `packages/core`/`shared`/`browser`. All ten are fixed and the built trees are at **zero**. | LS1.1a | L | **Done** | **Thirteen accumulations that silently do nothing** — The most consequential defect found in the whole migration, and **no lint rule reports it**. Each of these reads as an append or an increment and is a no-op `==` comparison in statement position:<br><br>**`privacy/placeholder.tk:41`** — `map.entries==str.arraypush(map.entries;entry)`. **The placeholder map never stores an entry.** Everything downstream of it — restoration, the mapping shown in the privacy review, PL1's collision work — rests on a table that is always empty.<br>**`governance/policy.tk:85,87`** — `controls==@(controls;"human-visible-preview";"cost-confirmation";…)`. **The controls a policy decision requires are never attached to it**, so a decision that should demand preview and cost confirmation demands nothing.<br>**`agents/registry.tk:42,188`** — `reg.agents==@(reg.agents;agent)` and `reg.runs==@(reg.runs;run)`. **Registering an agent never adds it to the registry**, and no run is ever recorded.<br>**`agents/sandbox.tk:139,158` and `agents/executor.tk:41,77`** — `sandbox.violations==@(sandbox.violations;msg)`. **No sandbox or executor violation is ever recorded.** With `recordcost` (also a no-op, fixed) that means the sandbox tracked neither cost nor violations — the two things it exists to track.<br>**`governance/use_cases.tk:126`** — use cases are never registered.<br>**`mcp/discovery.tk:122`** — discovered MCP servers are never added to the list.<br>**`memory/search.tk:93`** — keyword relevance never accumulates, so multi-keyword search ranks by insertion order.<br>**`moke/src/dashboard_flow.tk:167`** — appends a computed value into a card label; this one failing is a mercy, since that is the display-channel smuggling NC1.5 strips.<br><br>**Root cause.** All thirteen try to mutate a struct reached through a parameter. toke has value semantics: a callee cannot write to its caller's struct, so these were wrong before the `=`/`==` migration and it only changed how they fail. The fix per site is for the function to return the updated value and every caller to use it — not a one-character edit.<br><br>**What this means for the backlog.** Several of these sit under stories marked **Done**: the agent registry and sandbox (F7/GA-series), policy control attachment (GA1.8), MCP discovery, and the placeholder store. They belong in **RV1** as reopen candidates, and the placeholder one should be checked before any PL1 measurement is taken, because a recall figure computed against an always-empty map measures nothing.<br><br>**How to find them.** `([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_.]*)\s*==\s*(.*\1.*)` anchored to statement position. Note BSD `grep -E` silently matches nothing for a backreference, so use Python; my first sweep with grep reported zero and I believed it<br><br>**Status detail:** **Done in the built set; 11 more found in moke 2026-09-23** — 11 of 13 landed across `0b1f60e`, `eb6f166`, `67ca50e`. The F10.17a sweep then found **11 further sites, all in `packages/moke`**, which is not in the build: `ml/engine.tk:168,178` — the k-means centroid update is `sum.get(di2)==sum.get(di2) + …`, so centroid computation does nothing; `dashboard_flow.tk:150,167,224`; `ddl.tk:266,277,288,299`; `pages/api/agents.tk:86,121` — a discarded call result. They land with the moke onboarding wave. All are instances of upstream **T-26**: the compiler's own advice for a field assignment is `==` |
| LS1.1b | M | **Done** | **`x=@(x;item)` builds a nested pair, it does not append** — Verified by running: `@(a;4)` where `a` has three elements produces an array of length **2**, while `a.push(4)` produces length 4. So `@(arr;item)` is an array **literal** containing the array and the item.<br><br>135 sites across 32 files used it as an append: agent registries and run lists, overnight job queues, pipeline step results, quota rules, policy lists, CLI column widths, discovered servers. Every one built `[[[…], item]` instead of a growing list, so each accumulation produced a malformed nested structure rather than the collection its caller expected.<br><br>This is also why so many type errors read **"expected 'array', got 'array'"** — the two arrays differ in element type because one of them is nesting.<br><br>All 135 are converted. 117 went mechanically to `.push()`; the remaining 18 were multi-item literals and struct-field targets that needed the owning function to return the updated value — `agents/registry.tk`'s `register`/`recordrun` and `optimiser/profiler.tk`'s `extractcolvalues` among them.<br><br>Same root cause as LS1.1a: value semantics. `@(…)` constructs, `.push()` appends, and neither mutates the receiver |

Each also wrote through `.set(i;job)` and discarded the result, and returned `void`, so a terminating version would still have changed nothing.<br>**`models/registry.tk`** — `findfortask` always returned empty, `findbest` and `byid` always errored, and `register` had an **empty body**. Model selection could never succeed. `findbest` also took the first acceptable candidate rather than the best; it now keeps the highest quality score above the tier's speed floor.<br>**`models/ollama.tk`** — `listmodels` and `listrunning` always returned an empty list, so loke never saw a single installed model.<br>**`memory/shorthand.tk`** — `expand` and `expanddotchains` returned their input unchanged; no shorthand was ever expanded.<br>**`memory/mining.tk`** — all three counters stayed 0 and `mineall` collected nothing.<br>**`memory/mcp_server.tk`** — `handlesearchresults` and `handlefactsresults` were infinite loops returning `[]`.<br>**`memory/aaak.tk:encodetext`** — returned an empty document for every input.<br>**`memory/search.tk`** — `searchbuildsingleresult` took its accumulator as an `i64`, appended into a branch-local binding and returned `void`: **every memory search returned no results** however many drawers matched.<br>**`privacy/test_harness.tk`** — `runtestcase` threw away every assertion, and `formatresults` was an infinite loop. This is why AD1.8 found it excluded from `run_tests.sh`.<br>**`optimiser/profiler.tk:infercoltype`** — `allint==false` in a match arm is a discarded comparison, so every column typed as int.<br>**`optimiser/budget.tk:resetperiod`** — `manager.usage.get(i)==$budgetusage{…}` compared and discarded; no usage row was ever reset.<br>**`governance/policy.tk:classifyrisk`** — `basetier==$medium` discarded, so **a confidential request with a low use-case risk was never escalated.**<br>**`storage/dashboard.tk`** — widget preferences never persisted.<br>**`storage/ephemeral.tk:put`** — `json.set` returns a new object and the result was discarded, so **the ephemeral placeholder map stored nothing** and response restoration could never restore.<br>**`cli/proxy.tk:parseopts`** — every option returned its default and the loop never terminated.<br>**`cli/session.tk`**, **`cli/proxy_config.tk`**, **`browser/pages/installer.tk`** (always reported Ollama as not installed), **`browser/src/components/table.tk`** (JS `dir = 'ascending'` assigned rather than compared, so the sort direction was always ascending).<br><br>**Held by `scripts/find_shadowed_lets.py`**, scope-aware — two sequential loops each declaring their own `lv` are disjoint siblings and legal; the defect is specifically an inner declaration inside an outer one's braces. In the gate. |

Mechanism, for upstream: `llvm.c:1639` states the intent — *"Cross-module ABI: all non-void returns use i64 (pointers via ptrtoint, bools via zext, floats via bitcast)"* — and line 1641 passes a literal `"i64"`, discarding the correct `"double"` line 1629 computed; but the definition side (`resolve_llvm_type`, `llvm.c:680`) emits a real `double`, and neither side performs the promised bitcast. The callee returns in `xmm0`, the caller reads `rax`. The parameter is `double` on both sides, which is exactly why parameters cross intact<br><br>**Status detail:** **Re-sized 2026-09-23 by measurement, not by re-reading.** `tests/unit/shared/test_f64_boundary.tk` asserts all four arms; arm B is a tripwire that fails the day T-20 is fixed *Scope narrowed again 2026-09-23 by measurement: an `f64` **field of a struct** returned across a module boundary is **intact** — arms E and F of `tests/unit/shared/test_f64_boundary.tk`. Asked because `core.metrics.collector.summarise` returns a `$metricssummary` with four f64 fields that `loke stats` prints, so if they were corrupted every figure that command shows would be wrong. They are not. A struct crosses as a pointer, so the field is loaded from memory rather than read out of a return register — which is the same reason parameters are unaffected. **So this story covers bare f64 returns only**, and no struct needs migrating on T-20's account.* |

That pair is why this is gated rather than remembered: the identical misuse of the identical construct, fail-closed in one module and fail-open in the other, invisible to any test because the arms type-check either way. `scripts/find_err_arm_first.py` fails the gate on any result match whose `$ok` arm is not first; `scripts/fix_err_arm_first.py` reorders them, moving arm contents between slots and leaving each slot's whitespace alone so every hunk reads as a reordering rather than a rewrite. Both blank strings and comments length-preservingly — deleting them drifted the reported line numbers past the first comment and named one site 17 lines from its `mt`. |
| LS1.1o | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **`std.json` cannot read a nested value, and both hosted providers depended on it** — upstream **T-32**. The typed API is eight functions and every accessor takes a `Json` plus a FLAT key; there is no `json.obj(Json,str)`. All four routes measured: `json.str` on an object returns `$jsonerr`; a dotted path returns `$jsonerr`; `json.getval` then `json.i64` **SEGFAULTS**; `str.matchbracket` returns `$bracketerr`. The segfault is the instructive one — `getval` is one of ~50 names declared `(i64,i64)->i64` that reach a different representation through `json.parse` (T-17), and handing one the typed `Json` compiles and links because a one-field struct and a handle share an ABI (T-23). So the interface advertises ~60 functions of which 8 are usable, and a wrong pick is a segfault rather than a diagnostic. Consequence: both providers read token usage as `json.str(obj;"usage")`, so every audit row for a hosted request carried `tokens_in = 0`, `tokens_out = 0`, `cost_usd = 0` — in a table that is append-only and hash-chained over those three columns, so the zeros are permanent once written and the chain attests to them. OpenAI additionally returned EMPTY CONTENT for every completion, because `choices[0].message.content` is one level deeper than Anthropic's `content[0].text`. `packages/shared/src/jsonobj.tk` works around it on two things that do work: `Json` is declared `{raw:str}` and `o.raw` holds the whole document, and `$json{raw:…}` can be constructed in loke code and read correctly by the typed accessors. It brace-matches the object out of the raw text, tracking string state and backslash escapes, because a `}` inside a string value is data a provider really sends. `json.arr` IS typed and works, so array descent needs no trick. 16 assertions in `tests/unit/shared/test_jsonobj.tk`. |
| LS1.1p | S | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **Every numeric and boolean `std.json` accessor reports an error for a FALSY value** — upstream **T-33**. Measured with the field present: `json.i64` on `0`, `json.u64` on `0`, `json.f64` on `0.0` and `json.bool` on `false` all return `$jsonerr`; `json.str` on the empty string returns `$ok`, and it survives only because an empty string is still a non-null pointer. So a JSON zero cannot be distinguished from a missing key, and every error arm in a program is doing double duty as "the value was falsy". Fixed in loke rather than only reported: `jx.fieldiszero` asks the document text, exactly rather than heuristically — JSON forbids leading zeros in numbers, so a value whose first character is `0` is `0` or `0.0`, and a field that is present, not falsy and still errored is a genuine type mismatch that gets the caller's fallback. **This is probably T-18, T-31 and T-33 as one defect.** Eleven measurements are consistent with `X!E` being a single machine word carrying the value, with zero standing for the error and no tag at all — which predicts the falsy failure, positional arm binding, a user-declared error side always taking arm 0, `json.dec` on malformed input correctly discriminating because it returns NULL, and a bare `json.str` used as a `str` with no match working at all. Recorded in T-33 as inference from measurement, not as a claim about the compiler. If it holds, three issues are one fix, and it is the most valuable fix available in the language. |
| LS1.1q | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23 | **`~` is never expanded, and 29 path literals relied on it** — measured at toke `fedd48d` with the file genuinely present: `file.exists("~/.loke/probe")` is **false** while the absolute path is true, and `file.appendline("~/.loke/probe";"hello")` writes **nothing** — neither to `$HOME` nor to a literal `~` directory in the working directory; it fails and the result is discarded. The tilde is a shell convention and nothing in toke's path layer expands it. This is not an upstream ask — the path layer is behaving as documented; it is 29 call sites assuming a shell. **What it cost, in descending order.** `shared/log.tk` appended every `log.debug`/`info`/`warn`/`error` line to `"~/.loke/loke.log"`, so **loke has never written a log file**, at any level, for its whole existence — which is why nothing in this project has ever been diagnosed from a log. `shared/config.tk`'s `defaultconfig().datadir` was `"~/.loke"`, making the default store path a literal `./~/.loke/loke.db`. Three call sites — `browser/extensions/core.tk`, `cli/src/main.tk`, `mcp-broker/src/main.tk` — loaded `"~/.loke/config.toml"`, so **a user's configuration file was never read**; and because `cfg.loadconfig` returns `!$lokeerr`, whose discriminant toke never reads (T-18), the `$err` arm that was meant to fall back to defaults could not run either, so callers read fields off an error payload. `cli/src/doctor.tk` tested `file.exists("~/.loke")` and therefore reported the data directory and the config file **missing on a correct installation**. `core/agents/templates.tk` granted a sandbox permission scoped to `$readfile("~/.loke/")`, which can never match a real path, so the permission never granted. **Fixed** by `shared.types.homepath`/`lokedir`/`lokefile`/`configpath`, with `file.exists` guarding the config load so arm 0 is the right arm in practice rather than in principle. 24 sites converted across `shared`, `core`, `browser`, `cli` and `mcp-broker`; four left as-is because a literal `~` is the right thing to PRINT to a person (`loke doctor`'s report), allowlisted by exact literal in `scripts/find_tilde_path.py`'s `DISPLAY_SITES`. That sweep is in the gate. Remaining: one site in moke (MK21.14) and three in legacy `src/`, neither tree gated. |

It was also **not in the gate**. **Fixed:** all three handlers return their match. | LS1.2 | M | **Partial** — ratcheted, not migrated | **407 `string-concat-chain` → interpolation** — The token-efficiency win, and the one the catalogue measures most starkly. For a 3-part string: nested `concat` costs 20 proxy tokens, 95 minified bytes, 104 ms and 76,784 KB RSS; interpolation `"\(a)-\(b)-\(c)"` costs 13, 47, 82 ms and 39,136 KB. Roughly half the memory and a third fewer tokens, for the same output. 101 files. Prefer interpolation; `str.join` where the parts are already an array.<br><br>**2026-09-24: measured, ratcheted, and deliberately not migrated wholesale.** The real count is **866**, not 407 (LS1.5a). `string-concat-chain` has **no fix span**, so there is no auto-migration — all 866 are hand work. The rule's advice is sound: measured directly, `"\(a) \(b)"` and the nested `str.concat` equivalent both produce the same 11 characters for "hello world", and T-21's truncation is specific to `std.homedir()` rather than a general hazard of interpolation. **But there is no defect behind these 866 sites** — nested concat is correct, just unreadable — so a campaign to convert them is churn with a non-zero regression risk and no behavioural gain. What is in the gate instead is a **ratchet**: the style-diagnostic count may fall and must not rise, floor recorded in `baselines/lint.json` at **1530**. That is the same argument `build_census.py` makes for compile errors, and it needs no campaign to be effective — convert chains opportunistically when touching a file and the number only goes down. Remains Partial because the 866 are still there; it is no longer able to get worse. |

Cross-check each removal against `$typename` usage in the same file before deleting.<br><br>**Partial 2026-09-24, and the headline is that the obvious tool for this is unsafe.** `toke --lint --fix` deletes the imports it reports unused, and **that breaks the build**: an import is reported unused when its ALIAS never appears, but a TYPE is referred to without the alias — `$agentdef`, not `at.$agentdef` — so an import that exists only to bring types into scope is always reported unused. Measured on `agents/executor.tk`: `--fix` removed three imports and produced six `E3011 identifier not declared` errors for `sensitivitylevel` and `agentdef`. Across `packages/core`, **189** diagnostics of which **91 are type-only**, so a repo-wide `--fix` is not a cleanup, it is an outage. Filed as **T-39**. **A second case has no source-level test at all:** screening on "the module declares no type appearing as `$name` in this file" passed 96 removals, and **5 still broke the build** with `E4034 no struct in scope declares a field 'content'` — those imports were needed for FIELD RESOLUTION on a value whose type is inferred, so the type name never appears in the source. The only reliable test is to compile. **89 genuinely-unused imports removed across 68 files** in `packages/core`, established by building rather than by analysis, with the five offenders reverted. Build clean, suite 144 of 144. Remains Partial: the 91 type-only ones must not be touched while T-39 stands, and `cli`, `browser`, `mcp-broker`, `mcp-toke`, moke and legacy `src/` have not been through this — each needs the same remove-then-build cycle, which is cheap now that the method is known. This row's count of 232 was also wrong; see LS1.5a. |

All of it is now under the `baselines/lint.json` ratchet, so the count can fall and cannot rise. ## Epic RV1: Re-verify the Done Markers That Need a Binary

*The build was red for four months. Roughly 120 stories were marked Done by reading code.*

> **The problem, stated plainly.** A story marked Done for behaviour that has never executed is worse
> than one marked not-started: it is an assertion with no evidence, and this document is the input to
> `claims.md`, which is the input to everything the project says publicly. Two examples already found and
> corrected show the shape — the X4a.6 preamble asserted *"All 172 modules compile, 19/19 tests pass"*
> when 58 of 199 compiled, and GA2.2 was Done for SQLCipher encryption the toolchain cannot do at all.
>
> Every row in scope gets exactly one of three outcomes. **Holds** — exercised and true, with the
> command that showed it. **Reopened** — exercised and false. **Untestable** — names what is missing.
> "Done" without one of those three is not an acceptable result for any row here.
>
> **Amended 2026-09-23, after the first pass at RV1.4 found markers wrong in BOTH directions.**
> Three Done rows are false — **GA1.2**, **GA1.4**, **GA1.8**, each now Reopened with the code that
> disproves it. One open row is done — **LS1.1a**, 11 of 13 landed. So a ledger that can only reopen is
> not a reconciliation, and RV1.1a is added for the inverse direction.
>
> Two further amendments the first pass forced:
>
> - **RV1.1 is not 65 rows of equal weight.** Only 26 of 122 test files import a production module,
>   reaching 21 distinct ones. Every T1–T14 row whose subject is outside those 21 is unverifiable *by
>   construction* — the verdict is "untestable", takes a minute, and needs no code reading. Partition
>   before working it, or RV1.1 reads as 65 code reviews when most of it is a join.
> - **X7 is the method, RV1 is the ledger, and the unit of work is one story row — not one test file.**
>   De-stubbing `tests/unit/privacy/test_placeholder.tk` *is* how GA1.2 gets re-verified. Scheduling
>   X7.2 and RV1.4 apart means reading the same module twice and writing two verdicts that can disagree.
>
> **RV1.7 could not start with counts, because the table schema was broken. RV1.7a fixed it
> 2026-09-23.** Recorded so the shape is not reintroduced: splitting story rows on `|` gave 551 rows
> with 6 fields, **38 with 5** — G1.1–G4.6 and AG1.1–AG1.8 used a 3-column table with **no status
> column at all**, carrying status as a trailing `**[Done]**` inside the description — and 5 with 7 or
> 8, read as unescaped `|` inside a cell. Status values were inconsistent in form too: 347 `**Done**`,
> 72 bare `Done`, 82 blank, plus `Blocked — …`, `Queued behind …`, `**Reopened**` and free prose. That
> is why two careful parses of this file disagreed on its own row count (598/477 against 594/485).
>
> The repair also corrected that survey. The 3-column tables were eight, not five — M1, M2 and X5 as
> well as G1–G4 and AG1, 40 rows — and only one row, AG1.7, had genuinely unescaped `|`; the other four
> were already written `\|` and it was the splitter that was wrong. Four rows had no closing `|` at all
> (F10.7, F10.11, RS1, RS2), which GFM renders anyway. The schema is now gated by
> [`scripts/check_backlog.py`](../scripts/check_backlog.py) (RV1.7b), and the counts in **SUMMARY** are
> generated from the rows.

| Story | Size | Status | Summary |
|-------|------|--------|---------|
| RV1.1 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-23, all 65 rows carry a verdict | **The 65 T1–T14 rows** — Every one is marked Done under an epic preamble that demands runtime evidence: *"Tests verify runtime correctness — not just that files compile, but that functions return expected values for known inputs."* Several assert specific outcomes, e.g. T4.5 (*"gateway blocks RESTRICTED request when kill switch engaged"*), T10.1, T14.2 (*"POST to `/api/pipeline` with email in body returns anonymised prompt"*). Run them and record per-assertion results. Depends on the green build and on X7.<br><br>**Status detail — worked 2026-09-23.** All 65 rows now carry one of the three verdicts, and each verdict is written on its own row with the evidence. Result: **14 hold**, **51 reopened** — of which 37 cite a test file that is in `_archived-tests/` and therefore excluded from discovery, 12 cite a file that exists and imports nothing but `std.*`, and **2 were exercised against real code and found false** (T5.1, `core.storage.settings` binds the literal string `"now()"` into `updated_at`; T6.3, five assertions against `core.memory.decay`). The partition and its method are set out under **RV1.1 worked** above. `**Partial**` rather than Done because the 12 in bucket B become answerable as X7.2 de-stubs them, and each will then want a second look — this is the join, not the code review. |
| RV1.2 | M | **Done** — 2026-09-24 | **The X4a verification claims** — X4a.3 and X4a.4 (*"Verify loke/moke API handlers compile and execute"*), X4a.5.1–5.8 (each claims a named module now passes semantic checks — cheap to re-check against the current 141 failures), X4a.6.1–6.5 (live Ollama health, model list, `/api/pipeline` proxy) and X4a.7.1–7.4 (runtime CORS behaviour; X4a.7.4 is literally titled *"Verified implementation"*). X4a.4 is also the cited evidence for retiring BUG-7, so a failure reopens that too. **Done 2026-09-24.** Every claim named was re-checked against the tree or the running server. **X4a.3** was false until today: three API routes and three pages crashed the server on every request (F10.36, now fixed and guarded). **X4a.4 and X4a.6.5 are reopened**, because moke cannot link (MK21.16). **X4a.5.1–5.8** hold, because the loke build compiles all 224 modules with 0 errors. **X4a.6.2–6.4** hold for the offline case, measured against the running server. **X4a.7.1–7.4** (CORS) hold: an allow-listed origin is echoed in `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, a foreign origin gets no such header, and a preflight returns 204 with the allow headers. Checking CORS turned up something CORS does not cover. A hostile page's simple POST was still processed and forwarded to a provider. That is filed and fixed as GA2.6. |

> can settle them.

### RV1.1 worked, 2026-09-23 — the partition, and why it is a join rather than 65 code reviews

The epic preamble demands runtime evidence. **Sixty-five** rows carry a file path in their own
description, so the first question about each is not "is this behaviour correct" but "does the file
this row cites exist, and does it exercise anything". That is a join against the tree, and it
settles fifty-one of the sixty-five without reading any production code.

One mechanical criterion per bucket, applied uniformly to all 65 so the sweep is auditable and
reversible:

| Bucket | Criterion | Rows | Verdict |
|---|---|--:|---|
| **A** | the named file is **not in the live tree** — it is in `_archived-tests/`, which `scripts/run_tests.sh` excludes by an explicit `-not -path '*/_archived-tests/*'` | **37** | **untestable** — no run has ever exercised it, in CI or locally |
| **B** | the named file exists and passes, and imports nothing but `std.*` — it declares its subject inside the test file | **0** | **untestable** — it cannot fail when the module changes |
| **C** | the named file exists and imports production code — so it was compiled, linked and run | **28** | **holds** (24) or **reopened** (4) |

Bucket A is the sharpest single fact in this reconciliation: **37 of the 65 Done markers cite a test
file that no runner can see.** Not a file that fails, not a file that was weakened — a file the
discovery glob removes before compilation. T8.1–T8.10 name `tests/unit/platform/*`, T10.1–T10.5 name
`tests/unit/governance_src/*`, T11.1–T11.4 name `tests/unit/mcp_broker/*` and
`tests/unit/memory_src/*`, T12.1–T12.3 name `tests/unit/feedback/*` and `tests/unit/agents/…`, and
T14.1 and T14.3 name integration and e2e files that were archived with them. Each is now
**Reopened** — and the reason is stated on the row itself, because "Reopened" without it would read
as an assertion having been disproved, which is not what happened. What is missing is the test, and
it is named.

Bucket B started the day at 26 and **ended at zero**, because X7.2 was worked at the same time and on
the same files — which is the reason the two epics are one piece of work. De-stubbing
`tests/unit/privacy/test_placeholder.tk` **is** how T1.2 gets re-verified; scheduling them apart
would mean reading the same module twice and writing two verdicts that can disagree. Every row whose
named file is in the live tree now cites a test that imports the module it is about, so bucket C is
the whole of the answerable set.

Bucket C is where the value is, and it is where both directions appear. Twenty-four rows **hold**, each
with the command that showed it written on the row. Four are **reopened on a failing assertion against
real code**, not on paperwork:

| Row | Test | What is false |
|---|---|---|
| **T2.5** | `tests/unit/router/test_intent.tk` | intent classification always answers `chat` with `method:"llm"` and confidence 0.72, whatever the prompt, because all three of its stdlib-result matches are error-arm-first. `router.tk:308` feeds that task into model selection on every request |
| **T3.3** | `tests/unit/optimiser/test_toon.tk` | `measuresavings` returns a bare `f64` across a module boundary and is corrupted (T-20) — the returned values are denormals whose bit patterns are `str.len` of the second argument |
| **T5.1** | `tests/unit/storage/test_settings.tk` | all four setters bind `"now()"` as a parameter, so `updated_at` stores the seven characters `n-o-w-(-)` |
| **T6.3** | `tests/unit/memory/test_decay.tk` | five assertions against `core.memory.decay`, including `apply` returning `-inf` at every age — and `sweeprows` deletes below a floor |

A fifth would have been `tests/unit/governance/test_scorecard.tk`: its `countquery` defect was measured
and reported, another worker fixed it in source during the same session, and the test is green against
the repair. It is named here because it is the clearest single demonstration of the epic's value.

**The moke and CLI rows are untestable for two different reasons, and the difference matters.**
`packages/moke/` fails to compile — 52 `COMPILE_FAIL` lines over 42 source files in
`build/trees/moke/break-report.txt`, including every module moke's 33 tests are named after. The CLI
tree, by contrast, builds **clean** (zero `COMPILE_FAIL`) and is invisible only because
`scripts/run_tests.sh` passes a single `-I build/interfaces` and links `packages/browser/build/ooke`
alone; a hand-built probe against `build/trees/loke-cli/interfaces` links and runs `cli.commands`
successfully. One is a code problem, the other is a one-line runner change. See
[test-coverage.md](test-coverage.md) for both measurements.
